# Named addresses for the function oracle: `addr("name")` in a Rust test resolves through this file.
#
#   name                 program   address   notes
#
# One line per function you have proved or intend to prove. The program name matters as much as the
# address: an overlay's code shares its addresses with every other overlay, so an address with no
# program beside it is not an address. Keep the notes short and about *behaviour* — what the function
# computes, what its arguments are, what it reads from RAM — because that is what the next reader needs.
#
# This file is research, not product: addresses live here and in journal entries, never in the port.
#
# walk_ramp            gameplay  0x001000a0 speed ramp: (speed, stick) -> speed, args in f12/f13

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# R2 — the SDK and library functions. Also in kit.toml's [symbols], which is git-ignored: this file is
# the committed copy, so a fresh checkout can get the names back. `./kit fn @printf`, `./kit xref
# @snprintf`. How each one was identified is in research/journal/2026-10-15-r2-symbols.
#
# Output. printf first, because its call sites carry strings that say what the code around them is.
printf                      boot 0x003fe080 SDK printf(fmt,...): installs the TTY hook, then vprintf_core
printf_nohook               boot 0x003fe048 the same without swapping the hook; used inside the SDK
vprintf_core                boot 0x003fda58 (fmt, va) -> chars through the hook at 0x4223e4
tty_putchar                 boot 0x003fd778 printf's hook: buffer a char, flush the line at '\n'
tty_write                   boot 0x003fd1f8 the line flush behind tty_putchar
snprintf                    boot 0x001e6eb0 (buf, size, fmt, ...) — the game's own, 1850 call sites
vsnprintf                   boot 0x001e6f60 (buf, size, fmt, va)
vsnprintf_core              boot 0x001e5e80 the formatter behind both: (overflow_cb, state, fmt, va)
debug_printf                boot 0x00356580 DebugBuffer::Printf(obj, fmt, ...) -> snprintf 0x104 + append
debug_buffer_append         boot 0x00356680 appends a string to the growing DebugBuffer log
#
# Strings and memory: libc, hand-written with the R5900's MMI quadword ops. Oracle candidates, all of
# them: pure functions of their arguments and RAM.
memcpy                      boot 0x0040dfd8 (dst, src, n) -> dst; 32-byte, then 8-byte, then bytes
memset                      boot 0x0040e190 (dst, c, n) -> dst
memcmp                      boot 0x0040df40 (a, b, n) -> int
strlen                      boot 0x004112c8 (s) -> int
strcpy                      boot 0x00411130 (dst, src)
strncpy                     boot 0x00411890 (dst, src, n) -> dst, NUL-padded
strcat                      boot 0x00410d28 (dst, src) -> dst; finds the end, then tail-calls strcpy
strcmp                      boot 0x00410fe8 (a, b) -> int
strncmp                     boot 0x004116d8 (a, b, n) -> int
stricmp                     boot 0x00410c50 case-folds through the ctype table at 0x44aaa9
strnicmp                    boot 0x00411450 (a, b, n), same ctype table
strchr                      boot 0x00410e58 (s, c) -> char*
strstr                      boot 0x00411b28 (haystack, needle) -> char*
rand                        boot 0x0040fd40 newlib LCG: seed*0x5851f42d4c957f2d+1 >> 32 & 0x7fffffff
#
# The heap. Every public entry takes (…, __FILE__, __LINE__); the file names are strings in the image.
operator_new                boot 0x001e50b0 (size, file, line)
operator_new_array          boot 0x001e51a0 (size, file, line)
operator_delete             boot 0x001e5130 (ptr)
operator_delete_array       boot 0x001e5220 (ptr)
malloc                      boot 0x001e4e90 (size, file, line)
memalign                    boot 0x001e4ef0 (align, size, file, line)
realloc                     boot 0x001e4f60 (ptr, size, file, line)
free                        boot 0x001e5060 (ptr) — the pool index is the u16 at ptr-0xe
heap_alloc                  boot 0x001e3ec0 pool method behind malloc/new
heap_free                   boot 0x001e43d0 pool method behind free/delete
heap_realloc                boot 0x001e44f0 pool method behind realloc
libc_malloc                 boot 0x0040d408 the fallback used while the pools are not up yet
libc_free                   boot 0x0040d460
#
# SIF: the EE's side of every IOP service.
sceSifInitRpc               boot 0x003fe828 one-shot; sets the RPC queue up at 0x4e6180
sceSifBindRpc               boot 0x003fef68 (client, serve_id, mode)
sceSifCallRpc               boot 0x003ff138 (client, fno, mode, send, ssize, recv, rsize, end, arg)
sceSifCheckStatRpc          boot 0x003ff328 (client) -> 1 while the client's last call is still with the IOP
iop_rpc_send                boot 0x00198b90 (queue, fno, buf, size): waits on sceSifCheckStatRpc for the
#                                           other of its two buffers' clients, then a no-wait
#                                           sceSifCallRpc of this one; voice_start and the rest call it
SyncDCache                  boot 0x003fc8d8 (start, end), rounded to 64-byte lines, interrupts off
sceSifInitIopHeap           boot 0x00402f38 binds 0x80000003
sceSifAllocIopHeap          boot 0x00402fc0 (size) -> IOP address
sceSifFreeIopHeap           boot 0x00403128
sceSifLoadFileInit          boot 0x00403488 binds 0x80000006
sceSifLoadModuleBuffer      boot 0x00403c20 (iop_addr, arglen, args)
sceSifLoadStartModuleBuffer boot 0x00403c40 (iop_addr, arglen, args, &result)
sif_load_module_buffer      boot 0x00403650 the body both share: LOADFILE call 6
sif_fileio_init             boot 0x003ffeb0 binds 0x80000001 and the two fileio handlers
load_iop_module             boot 0x001f1cd0 PSXModuleUtils.cpp: read the IRX, alloc IOP RAM, DMA, load
#
# libpad. The per-port state lives at 0x4e9390, stride 0x70 per port and 0x1c per slot.
scePadInit                  boot 0x0041a758 binds padman 0x80000100 / 0x80000101, checks the version
scePadPortOpen              boot 0x0041a9b8 (port, slot, dma_buf) — dma_buf must be 64-byte aligned
scePadRead                  boot 0x0041ad08 (port, slot, data) -> length
scePadGetState              boot 0x0041ad88 (port, slot)
scePadGetReqState           boot 0x0041aea0 (port, slot)
scePadSetReqState           boot 0x0041ae38 (port, slot, state)
scePadInfoAct               boot 0x0041af28 (port, slot, actuator, cmd)
scePadInfoMode              boot 0x0041b168 (port, slot, term, offs)
scePadSetMainMode           boot 0x0041b2a0 (port, slot, mode, lock)
scePadSetActDirect          boot 0x0041b358 6 bytes straight into the pad's DMA buffer
scePadSetActAlign           boot 0x0041b418 (port, slot, align[6])
scePadSetButtonInfo         boot 0x0041b5a8 (port, slot, mask)
scePadInfoPressMode         boot 0x0041b658 true when the button mask is 0x3ffff
scePadEnterPressMode        boot 0x0041b6b8 = scePadSetButtonInfo(port, slot, 0xfff)
pad_button_info             boot 0x0041b4f0 the 32-bit button-capability word
pad_dma_buf                 boot 0x0041ac58 the newer of the two 0x80-byte buffers, dcache synced
pad_send_cmd                boot 0x0041a640 pushes the pad request over the SIF DMA
#
# libcdvd and libmc.
sceCdStRead                 boot 0x0041a260
sceCdStPause                boot 0x0041a3e8
sceCdStResume               boot 0x0041a438
sceCdStStat                 boot 0x0041a490
cd_stream_cmd               boot 0x0041a4d8 (…, cmd, buf): the stream commands all go through here
sceMcInit                   boot 0x0041b9d8
mcUdCheckNewCard            boot 0x0041c6b8
#
# libgraph and libdma.
sceGsResetGraph             boot 0x003f1360 the only GsPutIMR + SetGsCrt caller
sceDmaSync                  boot 0x003f1600 spins on CHCR.STR, then "libdma: sync timeout"
sceDmaReset                 boot 0x003f1780 zeroes CHCR/MADR/QWC on all ten channels
dma_addr_fixup              boot 0x003f16f8 0x7……… (scratchpad) -> 0x8………
#
# Not a library function, but R1 proved it and everything later hangs off it.
main                        boot 0x0016c720 the entry point's sole callee; installs the INTC handlers

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# R5 — the RNG. The game has no generator of its own: it calls the C library's `rand` from ~224 sites,
# and 199 of those scale the result by 2^-31 (0x30000000) to get a float in [0,1). How it was found is
# in research/journal/2026-10-15-r5-rng.
srand                       boot 0x0040fd28 (seed) -> void: the 64-bit state = seed, zero-extended
rand_state                  boot 0x00423b30 the LCG's 64-bit state word, inside the library's static
#                                           per-thread block (the pointer at 0x423d78 + 0xa8); the image
#                                           links it holding 1
random_list_pick            boot 0x001fed20 (list, out_index|0) -> list->items[i] where
#                                           i = (int)((float)list->count * (float)rand() * 2^-31);
#                                           list = {i32 count, i32 _, void *items}, i stored to out_index
#                                           if non-null. Two more instruction-identical instantiations
#                                           live at 0x003aaa30 and 0x003aaab0.
random_time_step            boot 0x00375810 (t, this) -> t + 7.0 * max(0.1, rand()*2^-31): the next time
#                                           in the chain of event times its one caller builds. Used as
#                                           the exact view of the random float in the R5 proof.

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# R6 — the live-object registry. There is no actor array: live objects hang off one singleton in
# linked lists whose nodes are {item, prev, next}, 12 bytes. The chain to the Nth actor, in full:
# [game_manager] -> +0x34 -> +0x34 (head) -> +0x8 xN (next) -> +0x0 (item). watch/actor-table.toml
# walks exactly that every frame; how it was proved is in research/journal/2026-10-15-r6-r6-actor-table.
#
# RAM addresses, not functions — the three that watch/actor-table.toml names. Also in kit.toml's
# [symbols]; this is the committed copy. frame_counter/frame_dt are R3's, repeated here because R6 is
# what put them in [symbols].
game_manager                boot 0x004e1e98 GameManager* singleton, null outside a level. +0x30 player
#                                           manager, +0x34 actor manager, +0x38 a third of the same
#                                           shape; each has {count, head, tail} at +0x30 and a second
#                                           list of per-type groups at +0x3c
frame_counter               boot 0x00479d04 the app object's own tick counter (app+0x374), +1 per tick
frame_dt                    boot 0x00479cd4 the dt handed to the simulation (app+0x344); 1/30 in play.
#                                           NB the vehicle's position does not advance by vel*dt — it
#                                           advances by the post-update velocity times a fixed 1/24 s
#
# The container, one template instantiated per manager (ContainerLinkedList.h).
actor_manager_ctor          boot 0x0021a780 GameActorManager::GameActorManager; vtable 0x00452ae0 at +8
actor_manager_dtor          boot 0x0021a7d0
actor_manager_add           boot 0x0021a8d0 Add(actor): append to the master list, append to the group
#                                           keyed on the u32 at actor+0x00 (1, 2 or 4), then store the
#                                           manager into actor+0x1c
list_append                 boot 0x0021b120 new node on the tail; 0x0021b2c0 is the second half
list_find                   boot 0x0021afe0 linear search of a list for an item
player_manager_add          boot 0x002459b0 the same machine for players: groups on player+0x34, stores
#                                           the manager at player+0x58, and also links the player into
#                                           the scene node tree via 0x00132f50
game_manager_ctor           boot 0x002462d0 builds the five sub-managers; its last store is the one to
#                                           game_manager above
#
# The three actor classes in a level. The actor record is a sub-object at +0x400 of its owner, so these
# constructors take the owner, and actor-0x400 is the object. Sizes are the operator new argument.
actor_class_monstertruck    boot 0x003deca0 type id 1, 0x1080 bytes, MXWorld.cpp:5272, vtable 0x0045ed50;
#                                           ctor takes the string at 0x0042ecb8, "MXMonsterTruck"
actor_class_type2           boot 0x00279ed0 type id 2, 0x14e0 bytes, MXWorld.cpp:4738, vtable 0x00454ef0
actor_class_vehicle         boot 0x00318c30 type id 4, 0x0c60 bytes, MXWorld.cpp:4885, vtable 0x00458f68;
#                                           the rideable one. Fields, relative to the actor record:
#                                           +0x00 type, +0x04 flags, +0x1c manager, +0x20 player,
#                                           +0x48 class vtable, +0xd0/+0xe0/+0xf0/+0x100 four rigid-body
#                                           points (x,y,z,1), +0x130 velocity, +0x180 4x4 world
#                                           transform with the position in row 3 (+0x1b0)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# O1 — the functions the oracle was proved on. Chosen for being too small to be wrong about: a handful
# of instructions each, no globals, no calls, no allocation, so a disagreement can only be the
# interpreter. Together they still cover the FPU accumulator chain, sqrt, max/min, VU0 macro mode,
# quadword loads and stores, out-parameters and one data-dependent loop. All of them are the game's own
# inlined maths helpers, emitted once per header and called from everywhere.
# crates/ps2-oracle/tests/basics.rs calls every one of these; how it was done is in
# research/journal/2026-10-15-o1-oracle.
#
# Scalars, in f12/f13, result in f0. One instruction each.
float_max                   boot 0x00107f80 max.s: (a, b) -> the larger. The game has no clamp of its
#                                           own — call sites compose this with float_min
float_min                   boot 0x00107f90 min.s: (a, b) -> the smaller
float_abs                   boot 0x00107fe0 abs.s: (x) -> |x|
#
# Vec3, by pointer. The quadword forms read and write all four lanes, so the w lane must hold something
# defined even when only xyz are wanted.
vec3_dot                    boot 0x00107ff0 (a, b) -> dot, as mula.s/madda.s/madd.s in index order
vec3_length                 boot 0x00108920 (v) -> sqrt of that same chain over (v, v)
vec3_length_sq              boot 0x0010b270 (v) -> the chain without the sqrt. Identical code also at
#                                           0x0010b260 (a thunk) and 0x0010b250 thunks vec3_length
vec3_add                    boot 0x00111e60 (out, a, b): three add.s, three swc1 — out may alias
vec3_sub                    boot 0x00111ea0 (out, a, b): the same with sub.s
vec3_scale                  boot 0x00108890 (out, v, s in f12): VU0 macro, vmulx.xyz off a broadcast of
#                                           s; w comes back from v untouched. 0x001088c0 is identical
vec3_normalize              boot 0x00108070 (out, v): vmul/vadda/vmadd for the square, then vrsqrt —
#                                           the VU approximation, not a correctly rounded one
vec4_abs                    boot 0x00108020 (out, v): builds 0x7fffffff in four lanes with lui/ori,
#                                           pextlw and pcpyld, then pand. All four lanes, w included
#
# A table lookup with a real loop.
keyframe_index_stride2      boot 0x001361e0 (t in f12, table, n) -> how many keys are at or before t:
#                                           a binary search over 8-byte entries keyed on the float at
#                                           +0, strictly increasing. Returns 0 below the first key and
#                                           n at or past the last. 0x00137550 is the same search over
#                                           0x28-byte entries

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# I1 — the input layer. The pad poll's timer update is not a function: it is a span inside the poll,
# so the test loads these instructions into a bare interpreter and appends its own `jr $ra`. The name
# is the span's first instruction; the length is in the test.
pad_timer_add_ms            boot 0x0016aef0 30 instructions, not a function: ($s2+0x330) += $f20 ms,
#                                           where the stored value is read back as *unsigned* (bltz,
#                                           then halve-convert-double) and the sum converted back as
#                                           unsigned (c.ole.s against 2^31, then subtract the bias and
#                                           set the sign bit by hand). Falls through to the span's end,
#                                           so the test's `jr $ra` sits where the poll's `b` went.
#                                           The identical sequence at 0x0016ae68 does +0x334 (held ms)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# I2 — the analogue axes. The poll's axis pass is a span inside 0x0016ab10 (three copies of it: the
# absent pad, the bad read, the good read); the arithmetic it dispatches to is a function of its own.
pad_axis_curve              boot 0x0011d2e0 (raw in f12, full in f13, consumer in a0) -> writes the
#                                           mapped axis to consumer+0x24: v = lo + (raw/full)*(hi-lo),
#                                           then the dead zone — centre inside it, and each side
#                                           stretched back to the full range. The consumer's floats are
#                                           +0x24 value, +0x28 centre, +0x2c lo, +0x30 hi, +0x34
#                                           dead_hi, +0x38 dead_lo. No clamp: raw is a report byte.
pad_axis_dispatch           boot 0x00134f50 (raw, full, pad, axis) walks the axis's consumer list at
#                                           pad+0x640+axis*0x18 and calls the curve on each
pad_axis_read_one           boot 0x0011d3d0 the single-axis reader: its own scePadRead, the stick byte
#                                           hardcoded per axis (0->6, 1->7, 2->4 inverted, 3->5), then
#                                           the same arithmetic inline. Not an oracle candidate — it
#                                           reads the pad through libpad
pad_poll_axes_centre        boot 0x0016aa40 centre every axis and release every control (pad closed)
# I6 -- the biplane's controls (R8 and C7 map the class; these are the names the input test calls).
#   In a ride the player's own four stick consumers (left x, left y, right x, right y: the player
#   record's +0x88..+0x94) carry centre 0, lo -1, hi 1, dead +-0.3 -- not I2's +-0.2, which is the main
#   menu's. Both gameplay states (first_recording on the bike, biplane on the plane) register those four
#   and no others. The port's AxisRange::PLAYER.
biplane_input               boot 0x00351e20 (x in f12, y in f13, plane a0, control table a1, pad a2):
#                                           +0x338/+0x33c = the values of commands 0x0e/0x0f; +0x340
#                                           = -sign(y)|y*(y*y)|, +0x344 the same of x; +0x348 = -1 for
#                                           0x14, else +1 for 0x15, else 0; then 0 (+1) / 1 (-1) over
#                                           +0x344 and 2 (+1) / 3 (-1) over +0x340. "Held" is value != 0.
#                                           Oracle-proved over the biplane state's RAM (tests/biplane.rs).
#                                           MXBiplane's own input slot (0x00355f40) is a bare j to it
#   The commands reach it through pad_control_index (named below): the binding map at manager+0x648,
#   kind 2 reads +0x138 + pad*0x138 + cmd*4 (+0x3a8 when sub is 6), kind 1 +0x618, kind 0x10 +0; -1 out
#   of range. Shipped table, both pads: 0x0e Cross, 0x0f Square, 0x14 L1, 0x15 R1, 0x00 Left, 0x01
#   Right, 0x02 Up, 0x03 Down.
pad_control_copy            boot 0x00134cc0 the pad's vtable +0x1c: memcpy of its control record
#                                           (pad+0x328 + slot*0x18, 0x18 bytes) into the player's table

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# O2 — the arithmetic carriers. No shipped game has a function that is one `div.s` and a `jr`, so the
# oracle coverage of F1-F3 runs each op through the smallest *call-free* function in the image that
# contains it: one or two operations wide, every input an argument or a field the test writes, every
# result still readable when the function returns. They were found by disassembling every call-free
# function and looking for the ops the O1 set never reaches. crates/ps2-float/tests/oracle.rs calls
# each one over 10k generated inputs from the awkward classes — zero and minus zero, both ends of the
# denormal range, the smallest normal, the largest magnitudes, the Inf and NaN patterns the EE has not
# got, powers of two, and *pairs* with a chosen exponent difference (0, 24, 25, 26 and more). How it
# was done, and what it cannot prove, is in research/journal/2026-10-15-o2-float-oracle.
#
# The scalar FPU. Offsets are into the object the carrier is a method of; the test writes the fields it
# reads, so no RAM image is needed.
float_mul_add_into          boot 0x0025d180 (obj, a in f12, b in f13): *(obj+0x6c) += b*a as a mul.s and
#                                           then a separate add.s, with the sum also left in f0. Both
#                                           factors are arguments, so this is the full-domain mul.s
float_recip_32767           boot 0x00388a50 (obj, x): obj+0x94 = x, obj+0x90 = 32767.0 / x. div.s with
#                                           a free divisor, zero and the denormals included
float_span_rate             boot 0x002f9ab0 (obj, a, b): obj+0x64 = max(a, obj+0x64); obj+0x6c = 0.5 *
#                                           that; obj+0x68 = (b - obj+0x5c) / obj+0x6c. div.s with a
#                                           free numerator, plus max.s, mul.s and sub.s in that order
float_inv_sqrt              boot 0x00284b80 (x) -> 1.0 / sqrt(x): one rsqrt.s against a lui-built one.
#                                           The only root carrier that takes its argument whole, so the
#                                           only one that can be given a negative or a denormal
quat_two_matrix_rows        boot 0x0013f380 (q, row_a, row_b): two rows of the rotation matrix of the
#                                           quaternion at q, as eleven mul.s, two add.s, three sub.s and
#                                           the accumulator pairs mula.s/msub.s (-> row_b+0) and
#                                           mula.s/madd.s (-> row_a+0). The only call-free msub.s in the
#                                           image whose result is not overwritten before the return
float_to_i16_pair           boot 0x00302ce0 (out, &a, &b): out[0] = (short)(int)a, out[1] the same for
#                                           b, as cvt.w.s + mfc1 + sh. Either half is skipped on a null
i16_pair_to_float           boot 0x00302d70 (src, &a, &b): a = (float)src[0], b = (float)src[1] from the
#                                           two shorts, as lh + mtc1 + cvt.s.w
#
# VU0 macro mode.
vec3_sub_vu                 boot 0x00107fb0 (out, a, b): vsub.xyz, bounced through the stack; out's w
#                                           lane comes back from a untouched
vec3_mul_vu                 boot 0x00108050 (out, a, b): vmul.xyz, lane by lane; w likewise from a
mat4_row3_add               boot 0x003f2e98 (out, m, v): copies m's first three rows as quadwords, then
#                                           out+0x30 = m+0x30 + v with vadd.xyz. The vadd carrier
vec3_div_scalar             boot 0x001088f0 (v, s in f12) -> v: v.xyz *= 1/s in place, as a vdiv of
#                                           vf0's w lane (the constant one) by s, vwaitq, then the
#                                           broadcast vmulq.xyz. NB `./kit dis` prints vdiv's two field
#                                           selectors the wrong way round — the encoding is fsf in bits
#                                           22:21 and ftf in 24:23, which is what the oracle reads
#
# Rejected carriers, written down so the next person does not look twice: 0x001346f0 builds a 2-D
# determinant with mula.s/msub.s and stores it to *a0, but overwrites it on the non-degenerate path;
# 0x0034ee10 reaches sqrt.s with a nearly free argument, but only through a pointer chain and two
# multiplies by its own constants. Nothing in the image feeds sqrt.s an argument of either sign.
# --- F3: VU0 macro mode ------------------------------------------------------
# Call sites that exercise the vu.rs model. The first two are small and pure and are the cheapest way
# to prove VU0's divider and its chop against the real code; the last two need the MAC flag, which
# ps2-oracle's interpreter does not keep yet (it has no cfc2/$vi17).
vu_scale3_recip        boot  0x001088f0 9 insns: Q = 1.0/f12 (vdiv), then the xyz of the vec4 at a0 *= Q (vmulq.xyz), stored back; returns a0. Proves vdiv + vmulq.
vu_unpack_s8_to_vec3   boot  0x001008e0 4 signed bytes at a0 -> vitof0.xyz -> vmulx.xyz by a scale looked up at (byte0 * 8) in a table; result stored at a1.
vu_quat_to_mat3        boot  0x0013ebd0 quaternion at a1 -> three matrix rows; vadd/vmul broadcasts and vmr32.w row, vf0 to put 0 in each row's w.
vu_plane_side_w        boot  0x003dc320 a dot product accumulated into .w, then cfc2 $vi17 and andi 0x10 (the MAC Sw bit) to branch on "behind the plane".
# F4 — the transcendentals the disc actually links. The float set is fdlibm's `float` flavour (Sun's
# freely-distributable libm, as shipped in the SDK's libm.a), compiled with every decimal constant
# rounded *toward zero*: S1 is 0xbe2aaaaa, one ulp below fdlibm's own 0xbe2aaaab, and so on through
# every polynomial. There is **no atan2f and no expf/logf in the image at all** — see the journal entry
# research/journal/2026-10-15-f4-libm for the full inventory and for what the game uses instead.
# `sqrtf` is not a function: all 305 square roots are an inlined `sqrt.s`.
# sinf, cosf, atanf, powf, fmodf and sqrtf are proved bit-exact against crates/ps2-float/src/libm.rs
# over every exponent an f32 has: cargo test -p ps2-oracle --test libm (52k inputs).
#
# The float set. One argument in f12 (two for powf/fmodf: f12, f13), result in f0.
sinf                        boot 0x0040aac0 fdlibm sinf: |x|<pi/4 -> kernel_sinf, else rem_pio2f + the
#                                           kernel the quadrant picks. Only 3 call sites — the game does
#                                           its angles with its own helpers, not with libm
cosf                        boot 0x0040a820 fdlibm cosf, the same shape
powf                        boot 0x00406d20 fdlibm __ieee754_powf. 0x0040abf0 is the thunk the game calls
fmodf                       boot 0x00406b50 fdlibm __ieee754_fmodf: exact, integer shift-and-subtract,
#                                           no rounding anywhere. Thunk at 0x0040abd8, 15 call sites
sqrtf                       boot 0x0011b880 the game's own one-instruction sqrtf: `sqrt.s $f0,$f12` and
#                                           nothing else. Every other square root in the image (305 of
#                                           them) is the same instruction inlined, so this *is* the
#                                           game's sqrtf, and it rounds to nearest like all EE divides
atanf                       boot 0x0040a478 fdlibm atanf, aT[0..10] from the table at 0x0044aa5c. One
#                                           call site. No atan2f to pair it with
#
# The float internals, exposed because a disagreement in sinf is always in one of these.
kernel_sinf                 boot 0x00409900 (x, y, iy) -> sin(x+y) for |x| <= pi/4; iy!=0 means y matters
kernel_cosf                 boot 0x00408f48 (x, y) -> cos(x+y), with the 1-z/2 split above |x|>0.3
rem_pio2f                   boot 0x00407500 (x, &y[2]) -> n, y0+y1 = x - n*pi/2. Exact to |x| <= 2^7*pi/2
kernel_rem_pio2f            boot 0x00409098 the big fixed-point reduction rem_pio2f falls back to:
#                                           (x[], &y[2], e0, nx, prec, two_over_pi). rem_pio2f passes
#                                           prec=2 and the table at 0x0044a420 (198 ints, the 8-bit
#                                           digits of 2/pi). init_jk = {4,7,9} at 0x0044a9b8, and the
#                                           eleven pieces of pi/2 at 0x0044a9c8 (six of them one ulp
#                                           below fdlibm's source, the toward-zero rounding again)
floorf                      boot 0x0040a920 fdlibm s_floorf, called by kernel_rem_pio2f
scalbnf                     boot 0x0040a9f8 fdlibm s_scalbnf, but with no gradual-underflow path: below
#                                           the smallest normal it saturates to +-0 through tiny*tiny,
#                                           because this FPU has no denormals to return
copysignf                   boot 0x0040a7e0 sign bit of y onto x
fabsf                       boot 0x0040a8f8 and.s with 0x7fffffff, out of line
#
# powf's tables: bp[2] = {1.0, 1.5} at 0x0044a390, dp_h[2] at 0x0044a3a0, dp_l[2] at 0x0044a3a8, and
# 0x0044a41c holds 0x7f7fffff, which the |y| > 2^27 shortcut returns where fdlibm's source returns
# huge*huge. Its over/underflow limits are the EE's: it gives up at 2^-126 and 2^129, where fdlibm's
# source says 2^-150 and 2^128. Everything in powf that tests for zero tests `< 0x00800000`, i.e. it
# calls a denormal zero; fmodf does the same with its divisor. That is the SDK's doing, not fdlibm's.
#
# The *double* set, for the record: the game calls these on float data through the compiler's implicit
# promotion, and the EE has no double FPU, so each one runs on libgcc-style soft float (correctly
# rounded IEEE754, round-to-nearest) — which means a Rust `f64` port of fdlibm matches them exactly.
# Not ported by F4; see its journal entry.
cos_double                  boot 0x00409a50 fdlibm cos, 3 call sites. Named the other way round until
#                                           S7 called both with a zero: this one answers 1.0
sin_double                  boot 0x0040a298 fdlibm sin, 2 call sites, and the one the curve table
#                                           builder calls second, for each entry's second word
tan_double                  boot 0x0040a3d0 fdlibm tan, 2 call sites
acos_double                 boot 0x00405548 fdlibm __ieee754_acos; thunk 0x0040aba8, 3 call sites
log_double                  boot 0x00405c70 fdlibm __ieee754_log; thunk 0x0040abc0, 10 call sites
sqrt_double                 boot 0x00406830 fdlibm __ieee754_sqrt, called by acos_double
ldexp_double                boot 0x00409e70 scalbn + the EDOM check; 27 call sites, the most used of all
rem_pio2_double             boot 0x00406278 the double argument reduction behind sin/cos/tan
# S1 — the fixed update. The frame is two functions: the game's tick services one mode request and then
# calls the engine's, which samples the clock, averages dt, reads the pads and runs the simulation.
# R3 traced the order, R4 the mode switch; S1 ported them and proves the arithmetic by calling
# `engine_tick` itself with its five indirect calls hooked. How, and what had to be patched to do it,
# is in research/journal/2026-10-15-s1-fixed-update.
game_tick                   boot 0x00201d00 MXGame::Tick(app) -> bool: the mode-request switch, then
#                                           engine_tick, then the quit flag. The loop's whole body.
engine_tick                 boot 0x00131100 Game::Tick(app) -> bool: clock, dt, pads, markers, the
#                                           simulation and the render. Not pure — see the journal for
#                                           the five hooks and the one patched `lui` that make it so.
engine_clock_prime          boot 0x00131800 (app): the first frame's clock, written to both ends of the
#                                           delta so the first dt is exactly zero
interrupts_off              boot 0x00404d20 `di` — wraps every read of the Timer 0 pair
interrupts_on               boot 0x00404d68 `ei`
display_ring_advance        boot 0x001a1c40 (gfx): steps the display-list buffer ring, end of frame
iop_command_flush           boot 0x00199b20 (queue): one RPC of the frame's queued commands to the IOP
#
# The engine's timing globals. Data, not functions: `addr()` resolves them the same way, and a test
# that builds a frame has to write all five.
dt_ring                     boot 0x0046a830 30 floats: the frame deltas the mean is taken over
dt_ring_index               boot 0x0046a8a8 where the next delta goes, 0..29
dt_ring_primed              boot 0x0046a8b0 0 = refill the whole ring from the next delta
fps30_cap                   boot 0x0046a808 set by MXGame's constructor: clamp dt to 0.03333
time_scale_object           boot 0x004e24c0 null normally; its +0x40 is the slow-motion factor
boot_countdown              boot 0x0046a7f8 frames until the one-shot boot object at 0x46a800 is freed
wall_ticks                  boot 0x0047b190 the 100 ms wall-tick count the clock's whole part comes from.
#                                           Read in eight places; nothing in the decompiled C writes it
#                                           — the Timer 0 interrupt handler R3 traced does.
app_pointer                 boot 0x0041f770 the one global pointer to the application object; the mode
#                                           switch reaches two of its fields through this instead of
#                                           through its own argument
display_object              boot 0x0048a560 pointer to the display singleton: the mode switch raises its
#                                           +0x2c on every front-end build, and the quit path flushes it
#
# The mode switch's eleven handlers, in the order `game_tick`'s cases call them. R4 named them from the
# `.cfg` parser and the heap API's __FILE__ arguments; S1 hooks every one of them, so what they *do* is
# still unproved here — only which of them a request calls, and on which frame.
mode_enter_world            boot 0x00202940 case 1 and case 6: build the world and its activity
mode_leave_world            boot 0x00202da0 cases 2 and 7: tear the world down
mode_build_front_end        boot 0x00202300 "Main" / "Initial": build a front-end page group
mode_tear_down_front_end    boot 0x002027b0 the counterpart, by group name
mode_reset_viewports        boot 0x00202010 case 2 only
mode_commit_event           boot 0x002b0850 case 6: commit the settings' event before the world builds
mode_shutdown               boot 0x00203160 case 8, and the only path out of the loop besides the flag
mode_loading_begin          boot 0x00202190 raise the loading screen
mode_loading_end            boot 0x00202270 drop it
mode_world_wait             boot 0x00211290 case 2: wait for the world before tearing it down
mode_ui_flush               boot 0x0022bd90 case 4: the boot-time memory-card page
float_to_double boot 0x00417b48
double_to_float boot 0x00418700
double_acos boot 0x0040aba8
double_cos boot 0x00409a50
double_sin boot 0x0040a298

# S10, the bike in the air. The five steps the bike's update runs for a jump, in the order it runs
# them, plus the globals and the one dependency they reach outside themselves.
bike_air_state boot 0x00251d20 (dt in f12, bike) -> which wheels touch, how long since they left the
#                              ground, and the crossup angle between the heading and the velocity
bike_preload boot 0x00256940 (dt in f12, bike) -> the stick-down-then-up preload: arms, fires, and
#                            adds its force to the chassis for PreloadTime
bike_backflip boot 0x00256b50 (dt in f12, bike) -> the stick-up-then-down backflip torque, its decay
#                             and the control-authority ramp it starts when it ends
bike_airborne_control boot 0x00256e50 (dt in f12, bike) -> the moment the stick commands while
#                                     airborne, the damping or auto-levelling, and the lean force
bike_wreck_test boot 0x002522c0 (bike, other) -> the whole wreck decision: g loads, pitch and roll
#                               limits, the landing crossup test, no-hander and joust
orientation_axes boot 0x001b24d0 (frame, relative_to, out_forward, out_up) -> copies a transform's
#                                two axis rows out as vec4s with w = 0: out_forward from frame+0xa0
#                                (the z row), out_up from frame+0x90 (the y row), each optionally
#                                brought into `relative_to`'s frame. With the two frames equal — which
#                                includes both being null — it answers the identity's two axes,
#                                (0,0,1,0) and (0,1,0,0), and does not dereference either. Hook it to
#                                choose a heading. S3's note called a1 an axis index and a3 the
#                                angles; both were wrong
acos_table_build boot 0x0012e150 () -> fills the 30000-entry acos table from the double routine
curve_table_build boot 0x0012e260 () -> fills the 6000-entry {cos, sin} table, 8 bytes each,
#                              indexed from -pi in steps of 2pi/5999
acos_table_ptr boot 0x0046a688 the 30000-entry acos table (a pointer, gp-relative in the code)
acos_table_scale boot 0x0046a6a0 14999.5: the acos table's index scale
curve_table_ptr boot 0x0046a7f0 the 6000-entry {cos, sin} table (a pointer)
curve_table_invstep boot 0x0046a698 954.77045 (= 5999/2pi): the curve table's index scale
wreck_enabled_flag boot 0x00420bc0 zero disables the whole wreck test
mem_alloc boot 0x001e4e90 (size, file, line) -> the game's malloc; hook it to hand the table
#                         builders a buffer, because the heap an oracle run starts with is empty
# S11 — tricks: the stunt table, the gesture that names one, the clocks, the landing. The port is
# crates/game-sim/src/trick.rs and the proofs are crates/game-sim/tests/trick.rs. How each one was
# found is in research/journal/2026-10-15-s11-s11-tricks.
#
# The shared arc-cosine table. Built once at start-up and read by ~70 places in the image; the trick
# direction is only one caller, which is why the port keeps the table and not just the decoder.
angle_table_build           boot 0x0012e150 () -> 1: allocates 30001 floats, fills them with
#                                           acos(i*step - 1) in double precision rounded to single,
#                                           and copies the last entry once more. Sets the two globals
#                                           below. Needs the heap, so a test builds the table itself
angle_table_ptr             boot 0x0046a688 the global the table is allocated into: a float*
angle_table_scale           boot 0x0046a6a0 the global 14999.5 the builder stores, read by the lookup
float_to_double             boot 0x00417b48 (f12) -> double in v0; the EE has no hardware doubles
acos_double_thunk           boot 0x0040aba8 (double in a0) -> acos in v0, software, ~9000 instructions
double_to_float             boot 0x00418700 (double in a0) -> f0
#
# The gesture.
stick_direction             boot 0x00251830 (x in f12, y in f13, obj in a0) -> direction 0..4 in v0,
#                                           writing the angle to obj+0x634 and the deflection to
#                                           obj+0x638. Reads the table through angle_table_ptr
pad_to_gesture              boot 0x00250fb0 (obj) the whole controller read: the three trick buttons
#                                           into the group at +0x9fc, the d-pad into the stick at
#                                           +0x628/+0x62c, then stick_direction into +0xa00. Reads the
#                                           input layer's control strengths, so not a pure function
#                                           Also the gas and brake levers: +0x63c is 1.0 or 0, and
#                                           +0x640 is 1.0 or 0 — except while the stoppie latch
#                                           (+0x728) is set, when a held brake is worth StoppieBrake
#                                           (+0x72c) instead. The only read of +0x72c with that
#                                           displacement anywhere in the program (S21)
stunt_request               boot 0x002589c0 (obj in a0, fallback id in a1) -> 1 if a trick is wanted
#                                           or running. The gate, the group/direction switch that
#                                           names one of the 24 slots, and the overlap test that lets
#                                           a queued trick take over the end of the running one
stunt_queue_push            boot 0x0025a5e0 (queue = obj+0xa38) push the named slot; heap nodes
stunt_queue_pop             boot 0x0025a570 (queue) -> the oldest slot; frees its node
stunt_ai_choice             boot 0x00280df0 (airtime in f12, obj in a0, out in a1) -> whether the AI
#                                           found a trick that fits: every slot whose duration fits
#                                           and whose name is not "throwaway", picked with rand()
ai_gesture_gate             boot 0x00280f30 (obj) the AI's wrapper: read the pad only when no trick is
#                                           being restarted and none is in progress
#
# The clocks and the landing. Both call the motion library through the rider's vtable, so neither is a
# pure function of RAM; both are proved anyway, by aiming the vtable at a table of hooked addresses. The
# clocks also need the call stopped at the end of the clock section (0x00259b00, the join every case of
# its switch reaches, where the animation selection begins), and the landing needs the attitude limits
# in front of the trick's clause put where nothing reaches them. See the journal.
stunt_clocks                boot 0x002593e0 (obj in a0, run the motion prologue in a1, dt in f12) the
#                                           per-frame machine:
#                                           restart test, the hold/ease/normal branches over elapsed
#                                           at +0xa1c and progress at +0xa2c, the no-hander test, and
#                                           the safe flag at +0xa10 against SafeTime and HoldTime
smooth_noise                boot 0x0013e810 (t in f12) -> the engine's shared 1-D smooth noise, from
#                                           tables built at start-up. Only perturbs the hold's phase
smooth_noise_build          boot 0x0013e900 () the tables smooth_noise reads, built off rand() on the
#                                           first sample (S41): per knot g1 (1 draw), g2 (2), g3 (3),
#                                           each (rand()%512-256)/256, g2/g3 normalised; then perm
#                                           shuffled i=255..1 with rand()%256; then 258 entries copied
#                                           up by 256. 1,791 draws. Port: game_sim::noise
smooth_noise_unbuilt        boot 0x0041f728 word: nonzero until the first sample builds the tables
smooth_noise_perm           boot 0x00476120 int[514]: the knots' permutation
smooth_noise_g1             boot 0x00479160 float[514]: the 1-D gradients
stunt_landing               boot 0x002522c0 (obj in a0, the thing it hit in a1, or 0) the wreck test: the attitude limits first, then the
#                                           trick's clause (reason 12 mid-trick, 7 for a throw-away
#                                           taken past 95%, 12 for a no-hander landed too slowly)
stunt_idle                  boot 0x00258cc0 (obj) what is requested when nothing was asked for:
#                                           airborne -> 25 (the stick-driven lean), grounded -> 28
stunt_state_pack            boot 0x00258d50 (obj) latches the requested id into +0x864 and the request
#                                           count into +0x8a0 — what a networked rider sends. Also
#                                           snapshots the body (S67): +0x830 = mat4_to_quat of the
#                                           node's world rows (+0x80), +0x840 the chassis velocity,
#                                           +0x850 the chassis' +0x280; stunt_clocks (a1 = 0 from
#                                           bike_vehicle_update) copies +0x830 back into the chassis
#                                           quaternion (+0x1a0), +0x840 into +0x260 and +0x8b4 into
#                                           +0x294, so every frame ends with the quat the node's
#                                           matrix holds (proved by the S67 replay test)
#
# The table on the disc.
stunts_ini_load             boot 0x0037d820 (obj, dir) reads <dir>Anim\stunts.ini: 57 [Stunt<n>]
#                                           sections of Name, DisplayName, SafeTime, HoldTime,
#                                           Duration, UsedForBike into 0x54-byte records, then writes
#                                           both players' identity maps at +0x12b4, stride 0x60
stunt_selection             boot 0x0037da60 (obj, player, out) fills out[0..23] with the records the
#                                           player's map names; the rider memcpys the result
stunt_specials              boot 0x00258790 (obj) finds "nohandedlander" and "throwaway" in the
#                                           rider's 24 by a case-insensitive name search, storing 24
#                                           when a name is absent
stunt_display_name          boot 0x0037dc90 (obj, player, id) -> the HUD name: 26 "Stoppie", 25
#                                           "Wheelie", 24 "BackFlip", else the mapped DisplayName
anim_rewind                 boot 0x001461e0 (anim in a0, 0, 0) snapshots the rider's current joint
#                                           poses into the motion object's two blend buffers, which
#                                           is what a restarted motion cross-fades *from* — it does
#                                           not rewind a clip. Called on every restart; hooked in the
#                                           tests, because it reads a real skeleton
clock_section_end           boot 0x00259b00 the join inside stunt_clocks where the clock section is
#                                           over and the animation selection begins: a test hooks it
#                                           to end the call before the tail's VU0 skinning work
wrecks_enabled              boot 0x00420bc0 the global stunt_landing returns immediately on when it is
#                                           zero: wrecks are switched off
#
# The rider's vtable, by the slots the trick code calls. The object's vtable pointer is at +0x48, and a
# test can point it at a table of hooked addresses.
#   +0xfc  wreck(reason)          4 pitched forward, 2/3 rolled over, 0xb landed at a bad attitude,
#                                 0xd thrown into something, 0xc the trick's own reason, 7 thrown off
#                                 a throw-away on purpose
#   +0x10c start the animation a trick id names
#   +0x110 stop the animation
#   +0x118 the hand-hold pose, on or off
#   +0x120 whether an animation is playing, which is what gates stunt_idle

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# S6 — animation driving: which motion plays per state, its frame advance and its blending. The port
# is crates/game-sim/src/motion.rs and the proofs are crates/game-sim/tests/motion.rs. How each one was
# found is in research/journal/2026-10-15-s6-s6-motion.
#
# The driving itself is the tail of stunt_clocks (above), past clock_section_end: the cross-fade ramp
# at +0x9f4 decayed by the rate at +0x9f8, the restart test, and a switch on the latched action id at
# +0xa78 that ends in exactly one of the four library entries below. The rider's clip per action id is
# the pointer array at +0xa88 — 24 gesture clips "bike_<stunt name>", then "bike_air_Fwd", "_Mid" and
# "_Bck" in the entries of ids 25, 26 and 27, which is why those ids' own arms never look a clip up.
# Fields the driving writes: +0x9ec the fore-aft lean mapped to 0..1, +0x9e8 the shown pose (read by
# stunt_state_pack), +0x9f0 "a cross-fade has run", and +0x788 the shown crossup — which is what
# stunt_landing judges a landing on, and the one output of this section that is not presentation.
# A test ends the call inside one of the four entries: past them the original skins the rider in VU0.
motion_advance              boot 0x00144800 (dt in f12, motion in a0, 0, 0, 0) the free-running path:
#                                           last = clip, prev = time, time += dt, the end test, then
#                                           the channels (or the transition's smooth step), then the
#                                           action frames, then the two resets. Does nothing at all
#                                           when +0x3c (finished) is set or +0x48 (the playing clip)
#                                           is null
motion_blend_one            boot 0x00146870 (phase in f12, cross-fade in f13, motion in a0, handle in
#                                           a1) the phase-driven path: clears +0x48, samples the clip
#                                           at phase * duration, and fires the action frames between
#                                           the last sample and this one
motion_blend_two            boot 0x00146ed0 (phase in f12, mix in f13, cross-fade in f14, motion in
#                                           a0, handle a in a1, handle b in a2) the same, with both
#                                           clips sampled at the same seconds of a's duration and
#                                           slerped by the mix: 0 is all b, 1 is all a. The action
#                                           frames come from a
motion_blend_three          boot 0x00147e40 (three weights in f12/f13/f14, cross-fade, motion, four
#                                           handles) two pairs, each mixed by f13, and the two results
#                                           mixed by f14. Not reachable from the rider's switch: the
#                                           two blocks Ghidra removes are in vehicle_animation, which
#                                           the S20 block at the end of this file describes
motion_transition_weighted  boot 0x00145810 (weight in f12, motion in a0, ...) the S6 name, kept so
#                                           the proofs that use it still resolve. It is the arm a
#                                           *null* +0x4c picks, so what it actually mixes is the pose
#                                           snapshot and the new clip: fade_from_pose below is the
#                                           same address under a name that says so
motion_transition_plain     boot 0x00145160 (weight in f12, motion in a0, ...) the other S6 name, and
#                                           also the wrong way round: this is the real clip-to-clip
#                                           cross-fade, the arm a non-null +0x4c picks. fade_from_clip
#                                           below is the same address. Neither is ported: the channel
#                                           sampling is the renderer's and the weight is proved
#                                           through the hook
fade_from_pose              boot 0x00145810 what motion_transition_weighted is: the new clip at +0x48,
#                                           sampled at +0x84 and weighted by the smooth step, over the
#                                           pose snapshot at +0x248/+0x24c
fade_from_clip              boot 0x00145160 what motion_transition_plain is: the new clip at +0x48 at
#                                           time +0x84 weighted w, the old clip at +0x4c at time +0x88
#                                           weighted 1-w, through anim_sample_channel and motion_slerp
#                                           (`./kit dis 0x144e20 0x144ea0` is the branch between them)
motion_events               boot 0x00144f70 (from in f12, to in f13, motion in a0, clip in a1, wrapped
#                                           in a2) the action frames one frame fires. Nothing when the
#                                           clip's key list at +0x14 is null; when wrapped, two scans
#                                           — (from, duration) then (0, to) — appended in that order
motion_event_window         boot 0x001363a0 (from in f12, to in f13, key list in a0, out in a1) the
#                                           scan itself: a binary search for the bracket, then the
#                                           *inclusive* test from <= key <= to over it. Keys are the
#                                           first word of a stride-8 entry at list+8, their action
#                                           lists at list+0xc, the count at list+0
new_array                   boot 0x001e51a0 operator new[](size, file, line) — the engine's heap. The
#                                           wrap branch of motion_events builds a second list with it,
#                                           so a test hooks it onto a bump allocator
delete_array                boot 0x001e5220 operator delete[](ptr), the other half of the same
motion_clip_over            boot 0x00140c60 (time in f12, clip in a0) -> duration (+0x3c) < time: the
#                                           end test, strict, so a time exactly on the duration is
#                                           still inside the clip and its last action frame fires
motion_slerp                boot 0x0013ee10 (weight in f12, out, from, to, 1) the quaternion blend both
#                                           blend entries mix their samples with. Not ported: the
#                                           channel sampling it works on is the renderer's
#   the motion object, by the offsets the advance touches: +0x3c finished, +0x48 the playing clip's
#   handle, +0x4c the clip a transition is coming out of, +0x50 the clip the last sample came
#   from, +0x54 that sample's time, +0x58 the event context, +0x80 a transition is running,
#   +0x84 the clip time, +0x88 the transition's clock, +0x8c when it is dropped, +0x90 its delay,
#   +0x94 its length
#   one clip, by the offsets the timing reads: +0x14 the key list, +0x20 rotation channels, +0x24
#   position channels, +0x28 flags (bit 0 loops), +0x3c the duration in seconds

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# R7 — the scripted freeride boot. Two words put the game into a loaded world with no front end:
# app+0x408 (StartMode) = 2 and the event's activity type = 5, with the event's league = 2. The event
# is `*( *(app+0x3d4) + 0xc )`; app is 0x00479990. `StartMode = FreeRide` sets type 0 instead, which
# loads nothing on a free* area (see activity_set_for_event below). How it was driven and what it
# proved is in research/journal/2026-10-15-r7-r7-freeride-boot.
#
# RAM addresses, not functions. Also in kit.toml's [symbols]; this is the committed copy.
start_mode                  boot 0x00479d98 app+0x408: 0 front end, 1 waypoint editor, 2 straight into
#                                           a loaded world. Written by the StartMode .cfg key and by
#                                           the TrackName autodetect; read by the boot state machine
mode_request                boot 0x00479d94 app+0x404: the front end's request to the main loop; 6 is
#                                           "tear the menus down and enter the world" (R4)
settings                    boot 0x00479d64 app+0x3d4: MXSettings*. +0x00 NumberOfRacers, +0x04 the
#                                           career/attract flag, +0x08 the viewport-mode object,
#                                           +0x0c the event
cfg_track_name              boot 0x00479d9c app+0x40c: the TrackName .cfg key, 0x40 bytes, copied into
#                                           event+8. "NONE" on retail — the disc ships no .cfg
scene                       boot 0x00479d5c app+0x3cc: null until a world is loaded; the cheapest
#                                           "is there a level yet" flag for a boot script
activity_manager            boot 0x004e1df8 MXActivityManager*; +0x50 the loaded world (track name at
#                                           +0x140), +0x58 the array of live activity objects
in_game                     boot 0x004e2650 1 once the world is running
all_ai                      boot 0x004e06b8 the AllAI knob: non-zero makes every rider AI. 0 on retail
#                                           and nothing in the xref index reads it — gp-relative
#
# The event, at *(settings+0xc): +0x00 league (0 supercross, 1 nationals, 2 free), +0x04 activity type
# (0 FreeRide, 1 Practice, 2 SingleRace, 3 GhostRace, 4 FastLapAttack, 5 Freestyle), +0x08 track name,
# +0x10c bike class (0..3 = 50/125/250/500), +0x110 tier (0 Race, 1 Pro), +0x114 players, +0x115 laps,
# +0x117 AI difficulty, +0x11c/+0x120/+0x124 the freestyle course record fields.
#
# The boot path.
game_configure              boot 0x00203260 MXGame::Configure(app, cfg): reads every .cfg key, each one
#                                           gated on a section the retail disc does not ship, so on
#                                           retail they all keep their defaults. Keys at 0x0042da10
#                                           TrackName, 0x0042da70 StartMode, 0x0042da28 NumberOfRacers
#                                           (-> settings+0), 0x0042daf8 AllAI (-> all_ai), 0x0042da38
#                                           NumberOfLaps (-> event+0x115), 0x0042da90 BikeNumber,
#                                           0x0042db00 EnableAIBowling
start_mode_from_name        boot 0x00203a70 (app, name) -> start_mode and the event's activity type.
#                                           FrontEndUI 0 · WaypointEditor 1 · Freestyle 2+league 2+
#                                           type 5 · None/Practice 2+type 1 · SingleRace 2+type 2 ·
#                                           FastLapAttack/fastlap 2+type 4 · GhostRace 2+type 3 ·
#                                           FreeRide 2+type 0
track_name_autodetect       boot 0x00203c70 (app): lowercases app+0x40c and derives the event from it —
#                                           "UI" -> start_mode 0; contains "free" -> start_mode 2,
#                                           league 2, type 5, and the freestyle course record's +0x24
#                                           into event+0x11c with +0x120 = 1, +0x124 = 0; "nat" ->
#                                           league 1 type 1; "sx" -> league 0 type 1. Always ends with
#                                           NumberOfRacers = 1. This is the authority on which fields a
#                                           freeride boot needs
activity_set_for_event      boot 0x00207580 kills the live activities, then picks the activity-set
#                                           resource from the event: type 0/1/2 -> "Nationals" if
#                                           league 1, "Supercross" if league 0, *nothing if league 2*;
#                                           3 "Ghost", 4 "FastLap", 5 "Freestyle". Loads
#                                           <prefix><name>, a runtime prefix first then "Tracks\"
#                                           (0x0041dc70). An empty name returns 0 and the load fails —
#                                           this is why league 2 only boots with type 5
track_res_load              boot 0x00205c40 builds the track resources with "%s%s.res" (0x0042def8) and
#                                           "Tracks\": Tracks\<TrackName>.res and
#                                           Tracks\<TrackName>_2.res ("_2.res" 0x0042df48), and for
#                                           split-screen freestyle the "%s_SS" form (0x0042def0)
#                                           first. The disc's archive has free06/free07/free09/free10/
#                                           free11 as .res, _2.res, _ss.res and _ss_2.res, each with a
#                                           UI\free<nn>.lsi loading screen; the ELF names only
#                                           "Free10" (0x0043d898) and "Free06" (0x0043d8a0)
#
# The hit/stunt activity — the class R4 left unnamed. One class behind two names.
activity_factory            boot 0x0021b880 MXActivityManager's create-by-name (vtable 0x00452bf4): a
#                                           chain of strcmps over the names in the activity-set
#                                           resource. "HitMaker" (0x0042f710) or "HitFreeride"
#                                           (0x0042f720) -> operator new(0x1a0, MXActivityManager.cpp,
#                                           line 203) -> hit_freeride_ctor, stored at
#                                           *(activity_manager+0x58) + 0xc
hit_freeride_ctor           boot 0x00330460 HitFreeride::HitFreeride(obj, 0): names itself from
#                                           0x0043d850 "HitFreeride", vtables 0x00459da0 at +0x44,
#                                           0x00459dec at +0x58, 0x00459e60 at +0x17c; zeroes +0x160..
#                                           +0x17c, +0xb4 = 3, +0xb0 = 1, +0x144 = 0.5f, flags at +0xac
#                                           |= 0x14000. 0x1a0 bytes
hit_freeride_init           boot 0x00330620 the 8-byte init the factory calls after the constructor
hit_freeride_attach         boot 0x00330630 the vtable's per-actor attach (vtable slot at 0x00459db4):
#                                           binds RaceStatusVehicleInterface (0x0043d860) and, for a
#                                           player vehicle, MXVehicleAIInterface (0x0043d880), then
#                                           compares the loaded track name (*(activity_manager+0x50) +
#                                           0x140) with "Free10" and "Free06" — bit 0x40 of the AI
#                                           interface's flags at +0x30 is SET on those two areas and
#                                           cleared on every other track. (This line said the opposite
#                                           until S14; the MIPS at 0x003307b0..0x00330818 has
#                                           `ori $2,$2,64` on the match path and an `and` against -65
#                                           on the else path.) The bit is read with the per-tick bit
#                                           0x80 by the two AI update paths below.

# The race activities (S13, stretch). Moved here out of plan/TODO.md, which is committed and must not
# carry addresses; kit.toml's [symbols] is untracked, so this file is the only committed record.
activity_supercross         boot 0x002f9b70 the "Supercross" / "Nationals" activity class, 0x4e0 bytes

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# D7 — which bike a freeride area loads. The vehicle build loader names two physics files from the
# event's own two fields, and nothing on the freeride path writes them, so a freeride bike is the
# event constructor's default: class 1 (125), tier 0 (Race). What was measured is in
# research/journal/2026-10-15-d7-d7-freeride-bike-class.
vehicle_build_load          boot 0x00208c50 builds a vehicle from the event: reads tier (event+0x110,
#                                           0 Race else Pro) and class (event+0x10c, 0..3 =
#                                           50/125/250/500) and snprintfs the pair of physics names
#                                           into two 0x104-byte buffers — the P name first, then the A
#                                           name — from the sixteen formats at 0x0042e0d8..0x0042e1c8
#                                           ("%sRace50P.phy" … "%sPro500A.phy", P before A inside each
#                                           class, the eight Race before the eight Pro), with %s =
#                                           path_prefix_bike. Activity type 5 also loads
#                                           "machine.res" (0x0042e0c8) here; no mode changes the pair
path_prefix_bike            boot 0x004e2000 the 0x104-byte prefix every bike file name is built on;
#                                           "Bike\" (0x0042d838), so the loader asks for
#                                           "Bike\Race125P.phy". The archives spell their members
#                                           lowercased, so the lookup folds case somewhere
path_prefix_init            boot 0x00200df0 fills the four vehicle path prefixes once at startup:
#                                           0x004e2000 "Bike\", 0x004e1eb0 "Rider\", 0x004e2540
#                                           "Biplane\", 0x004e2670 "helicopter\"
settings_make_event         boot 0x002b20c0 MXSettings' init: operator new(0x148, MXSettings.cpp) ->
#                                           event_ctor, stored at settings+0xc, then league = 0,
#                                           type = 2 and the first track record's name into event+8
event_ctor                  boot 0x002b0780 MXEvent::MXEvent, 0x148 bytes — the defaults a boot that
#                                           configures nothing rides on: +0x10c = 1 (125cc),
#                                           +0x110 = 0 (Race), +0x114 = 1 player, +0x115 = 5 laps,
#                                           +0x117 = 0x5a AI, +0x11c/+0x120/+0x124 = 6/0/4, league 0,
#                                           type 1 (the init above overwrites those two)
#
# The only two things that write the bike fields, and why neither fires on a retail freeride boot:
#   game_configure (0x00203260) reads the .cfg keys "BikeClass" (0x0042db88; "50"/"125"/"250"/"500" ->
#   0/1/2/3, default "250" = 2) and "PhysicsModel" (0x0042db70; "Race" -> 0, "Pro" -> 1, default
#   "Race"), both gated on a section the retail disc does not ship — those defaults are where the
#   port's old 250 guess came from. settings_create (0x00203dc0) forces class 2 right after building
#   the settings object, but only when the word at 0x004e2240 is 2.
kiosk_mode                  boot 0x004e2240 a build/kiosk switch in .bss: 0 on retail (both RAM images
#                                           D7 read), and all 38 references in the dump are compares,
#                                           none a store. 2 forces the 250 class; 2 and 3 also pick
#                                           alternate front-end and loader paths

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# F5 — the matrix and quaternion helpers for bones and cameras. The port is
# crates/game-sim/src/math.rs and the proofs are crates/game-sim/tests/math.rs; how each one was read
# is in research/journal/2026-10-15-f5-f5-matrix. Two of them are already listed above under their
# F1-F3 names and are not repeated here: `vu_quat_to_mat3` (0x0013ebd0) is the quaternion-and-position
# to 4x4 builder — it writes *four* rows, the position with w = 1 into row 3 — and
# `quat_two_matrix_rows` (0x0013f380) is the scalar pair of rows, a1 the forward axis (matrix row 2)
# and a2 the up axis (row 1).
#
# The 4x4 product is inlined everywhere and has no function of its own, so the carrier is the smallest
# call-free function that contains one. Note it overwrites two lanes of its right-hand matrix before
# loading it -- obj+0x68 and obj+0x78 are obj+0x40's lanes [2][2] and [3][2], and they get
# 1/(1 - f12/f13) and its negative times f12 -- so a test must read the matrix back out of RAM after
# the call and give *that* to the port. Generating f12 and f13 keeps those two lanes varying.
mat4_mul_carrier       boot 0x001131d0 (obj, f12, f13): obj+0xc0 = obj+0x80 * obj+0x40, four
#                                      vmulax/vmadday/vmaddaz/vmaddw chains, one per row of the left
#                                      matrix. Also writes obj+0x68, +0x78, +0x19c and +0x1a0
mat4_transform_points  boot 0x0013e3f0 (out, src, mat, n, out_stride, in_stride): n points through
#                                      mat, all four lanes out, the input's w lane replaced by 1.0
#                                      first (vaddx.w off vf0). in_stride 0 means "same as out_stride";
#                                      n <= 0 does nothing
mat4_basis             boot 0x0013e330 (out, pos, forward, up, orthogonalise, keep_forward): the
#                                      look-at. row1 = normalised up, row2 = normalised forward, row0
#                                      their cross, row3 = pos with w = 1, the other three w = 0. With
#                                      orthogonalise set, right = up x forward first and then either
#                                      up = forward x right (keep_forward) or forward = right x up.
#                                      Both normalisations are vrsqrt, the approximate one
quat_deriv             boot 0x0013ec40 (out, q, omega): 0.5*(q (x) omega) for a pure omega, as
#                                      vopmula/vmaddaw/vopmsub for the vector part and three
#                                      separately-halved products for the scalar. The half comes from
#                                      a constant quadword (0.5, 0.5, 0.5, -0.5), so the w lane is not
#                                      a dot product halved
quat_slerp             boot 0x0013ee10 (out, a, b, t in f12, shortest): spherical interpolation.
#                                      Returns a unchanged when |dot4(a, b)| >= 1 — it branches over
#                                      its own store. The angle is the shared acos table
#                                      (acos_table_ptr/-_scale above) read as lo - w*lo + w*hi through
#                                      the FPU accumulator, which is a different order from the
#                                      crossup's lookup; the three sines are a 7th-order Taylor
#                                      polynomial on VU0, three lanes at once, constants at 0x00426e10
mat4_to_quat           boot 0x0013f7b0 (out, mat): the rotation back to a quaternion by largest of the
#                                      four 0.25*(1 +- m00 +- m11 +- m22) candidates, each compared
#                                      with <= so a tie loses. Scalar FPU: sqrt.s then 0.25/it
mat4_affine_inverse    boot 0x001e7180 (out, m) -> det != 0: the general affine inverse. The 3x3
#                                      determinant on the FPU (mula/msub for the minors, suba/madd for
#                                      the sum), out left untouched when it is 0; else the adjugate below
#                                      into out, then div.s 1/det and vmulx.xyzw on each of the four
#                                      rows. Ported as math::inverse, proved in tests/math.rs (S107)
mat4_affine_adjugate   boot 0x001e6fd0 (out, m, transpose): the cofactors, FPU only, with column 3
#                                      read as (0, 0, 0, 1); flag 1 stores them transposed. Its zeros are
#                                      negated products, so a diagonal tensor's off-diagonals come out -0
chassis_set_inertia    boot 0x00158e10 the chassis vtable's +0x7c (vtable 0x0044e8f0): copies the tensor
#                                      to ch+0xb0 and tail-calls mat4_affine_inverse(ch+0xf0, ch+0xb0).
#                                      Run at load only (no frame writes +0xf0). race125p's 1/18.4 is
#                                      0x3d5e9bd3 here where a reciprocal gives 0x3d5e9bd4 (S107)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# S4 — collision against the world. The sweep and the shape test, as the sphere/probe-bundle class
# runs them against a collision mesh. crates/game-sim/src/collide.rs is the port and
# crates/game-sim/tests/collide.rs the proof; research/journal/2026-10-15-s4-s4-collision has the
# reconnaissance, including the classes and the call chain that reaches this.
mesh_sweep                  boot 0x0010f460 the entry the mesh classes call: stashes the vertex
#                                           descriptor in the global at 0x00468a78, shifts the
#                                           remaining arguments down and tail-calls the sweep below.
#                                           (bundle, child word, &{nodes, triangles}, descriptor,
#                                           record, 1) with the parent's live-probe mask in t1 — 0 at
#                                           the top call
mesh_sweep_level            boot 0x0010cfa0 one level of the descent, and the leaf test. Recursive:
#                                           bumps PTR 0x0041daa0 (the scratchpad cursor, 0x70000000)
#                                           by 0x40, copies the parent's sixteen live-probe words into
#                                           its own block (0x0041dab0 = all ones for the top level),
#                                           then either tests the node's box or the leaf triangle.
#                                           Node: whole bundle first (|centre - bundle centre| <= half
#                                           + radius per axis), then the first live probe that reaches
#                                           the box descends *both* children and the probes before it
#                                           are switched off for the subtree. Leaf: cross product of
#                                           the two edges, vec3_normalize, backface cull (normal.dir
#                                           must be < 0), t = normal.(v0 - origin)/normal.dir inside
#                                           0..length, then the containment test — flatten onto the two
#                                           axes the normal is weakest on, s = cross ratio to the edge
#                                           v1->v2, inside is s >= 1 and 0 <= u <= 1. Always 16 probes
#                                           and 256 levels, both because of where the mask lives
mesh_triangle_vertices      boot 0x001080d0 (out0, out1, out2, triangle, descriptor): the three
#                                           vertices by index, w = 1.0. Descriptor is {array, per-
#                                           vertex normals or 0, packed}; wide is three floats at
#                                           index*0xc, packed three shorts at index*6 scaled by
#                                           0x38000100 (a rounded 1/32767) and then by 32767.0
#                                           (0x004206d8) — in that order, which is not what the
#                                           decompilation prints
#
# In memory (not on the disc): a tree node is 0x24 bytes — key at +0, |half| at +4, child words at +8
# and +0xa (top bit = leaf), centre at +0xc, half extents at +0x18; a triangle is 0x14 with its three
# u16 vertex indices at +0. The collision record, as the sweep uses it: +0x20 nearest normal, +0x30
# nearest point, +0x40 the push-out, +0x8c the hit tally, +0x94/+0x98/+0xa0/+0xa4/+0xa8 the per-probe
# normal/point/owner/flag/fraction arrays, +0xb0 the nearest fraction (cleared to 1.0). A probe bundle:
# +0x120 count, +0x12c/+0x130/+0x134 the origin/direction/length arrays, +0x140 centre, +0x160 radius.
#
# The rest of the subsystem, read but not ported here:
sphere_collide_pair         boot 0x001e9540 the sphere/probe class's per-pair test: rejects on the
#                                           bundle's sphere, dispatches on the other object's class at
#                                           +0x68 (0 mesh, 1 hierarchy, 2 rays, 3 spheres, 8 finite
#                                           plane), transforms the probes into mesh space, calls
#                                           mesh_sweep, transforms the results back
ray_collide_pair            boot 0x001ebe70 the ray class's per-pair test, which is what a bike's
#                                           wheels use: a *tree against a tree*, set up by 0x00109130
#                                           and descended by 0x00108980 (node/node 0x00106000,
#                                           node/leaf 0x001079d0, leaf/leaf 0x00108340, segment vs
#                                           triangle 0x0010b980). Its record form keeps a contact list
#                                           (capacity at +0x88, count at +0x8c) and resolves a hit as
#                                           min + max of the per-contact push-outs (+0x50 and +0x60
#                                           into +0x40)
collide_against_search      boot 0x0011a9d0 (object, search): vtable +0x90 clears the record, then
#                                           +0x94 per candidate the broad phase gathered; sets bit
#                                           0x20 of the object's flags on a hit and fires listeners
collision_search_gather     boot 0x00218b80 the broad phase: up to 0x100 candidates (the count the
#                                           bike's own search is built with at 0x00252ff0), each with
#                                           its distance, re-sorted only when the object has moved

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# S5 — the main actor's state machine: riding, down, waiting, put back. The port is
# crates/game-sim/src/state.rs and the proofs are crates/game-sim/tests/state.rs. The player bike is
# type 2 (ctor 0x00279ed0, 0x14e0 bytes, vtable 0x00454ef0); the base vehicle's vtable is 0x00454560.
# Slots on it: +0xe0 update (base 0x002520d0, player 0x0027c890), +0xe8 the hand-reset chord, +0xf8 the
# ride state machine, +0xfc the wreck entry, +0x100 the unwreck, +0x248 the respawn.
ride_state_machine          boot 0x0027bc90 (dt in f12, bike) the player bike's slot +0xf8: the whole
#                                           ride state machine, run last in the vehicle update. Fields
#                                           on the bike: +0x748 reason (0 = riding), +0x74c the reset
#                                           latch, +0x794 the clock, +0x798 MaxWreckTime, +0x79c
#                                           CannonballWreckTime, +0xd3c the settle gate, +0x10d0 the AI
#                                           route (+0xd4 = following), +0x20 the player record (+0x34
#                                           control source, 1 = local human; +0x50 its screen), +0xf9c
