# chain in an order Ghidra reorders, and one of them calls a function with seven register arguments,
# which Ghidra prints as a call with two.
#
# bike_stand_upright 0x002574e0 — the low-speed upright controller. Only with both wheels down, not
#   balancing, no throttle and |speed| < 10 (a literal, not a key). It takes the part of gravity that
#   runs along the ground — gravity less its component along the front wheel's contact normal — splits
#   that into the part along the chassis' forward axis flattened into the contact plane and the part
#   across it, and pushes back: minus the across part always, minus the along part as well while the
#   brake is held. The forward axis comes from orientation_axes against the chassis' own transform,
#   which may be null; the function does not check, and the null answer is (0,0,1,0). A flattened
#   forward of exactly zero length makes it give up and add nothing.
# bike_balance 0x002561f0 — balance on one wheel. Only with the front wheel in contact. Four
#   accumulators, all of them at +0xa4c..+0xa6f and all of them zeroed by the step whenever the bike
#   is not balancing (S3 read that clearing as the no-hander's; it is this one's):
#     +0xa4c balance_inertia      the rate the rider's input is let through, ramped by
#                                 BalanceInertiaRate between MinBalanceInertia and MaxBalanceInertia
#     +0xa60 balance_command      the command the stick and the noise make, clamped to +-1
#     +0xa64 balance_point        the lean the command integrates to, clamped to +-MaxBalancePoint
#     +0xa6c balance_noise_time   the noise's own clock
#   The roll angle it controls to comes from the front wheel's contact normal (front+0x130) brought
#   into the bike node's frame, flattened into the frame's xy plane, normalised, and read off the acos
#   table — the same lookup air.rs::AcosTable::at already is. The controller is a P term on that angle
#   less the roll rate (chassis+0x290's z) and less the disturbance the balance point commands, and
#   what it moves is the chassis' centre of gravity in x, clamped to +-RollControlMaxCG.
# bike_roll_pitch 0x00256470 — the roll and pitch the rider's lean feeds, on the ground only. Two
#   halves, each a PI controller on a pitch rate with the integral at +0x724:
#     the wheelie — rear wheel down, throttle on, speed over 5 (a literal), the *rear* brake off
#       (+0x654), no clutch, and the stick pulled back past 0.5. The command fades out with speed
#       through +0x720's 1/WheelieTopSpeed.
#     the stoppie — latched at +0x728 by the *front* brake (+0x650) and the stick pushed forward past
#       -1, and dropped
#       again below -0.1 of stick or under 7 of speed. It adds a yaw term as well:
#       1000 * (StoppieYawRateScale * lean - the rate about the rear contact normal).
#   The wheelie half measures the same rate against the same normal and multiplies the result by a
#   literal zero, so that whole dot product is dead.
# bike_no_hander 0x00255d20 — rolls the bike node about its own forward axis so the rider can let go.
#   The angle it rolls towards is the ground's roll angle under whichever wheels are properly down
#   (a ray hit, or a swept contact whose normal is more than 0.17 up), faded out with speed, with the
#   rider's lean commanding a roll up to MaxRollCommand — widened by DonutRollAngleScale while the
#   donut is on — and RollGain * dt of first-order lag on the way. It ends in transform_set_axes.
transform_set_axes boot 0x001b1e20 (node, relative_to, forward, up, sync_a in t0, sync_b in t1,
#                                  sync_c in t2) -> builds the node's world matrix from two axes by
#                                  Gram-Schmidt: row0 (+0x40) = forward x up, row1 (+0x90) = up,
#                                  row2 (+0xa0) = forward, row3 the translation it keeps. The pair
#                                  orientation_axes reads back out, which is how a2 is known to be the
#                                  forward (z) axis and a3 the up (y) axis. Ghidra prints the
#                                  orthonormalising callee (0x0013e330) as a two-argument call; it
#                                  takes five, in a2, a3, t0, t1 and t2, passed straight through
# P32: transform_set_axes' one callee is mat4_basis (0x0013e330, the port's math::basis, proved), at
#   the node's own translation (+0x70..+0x78), with orthogonalise = t0 = 1 and keep_forward = t1 = 1
#   (./kit dis 0x001b1e20). So the "Gram-Schmidt" above is mat4_basis, not a cross of its own; the
#   port's ride.rs set_axes is now that call.
# P32: the node bike_no_hander writes is *(bike+0x18), which is the chassis' transform +0x254 (see
#   bike_attach_nodes below), and chassis_integrate (0x0015c540) begins by turning that transform's
#   rotation into a quaternion and folding the change since its own +0x1a0 into the body. So the
#   no-hander's roll is the body's roll: the lean a grounded bike shows is the chassis', although
#   ang_free pins its roll *rate*. Checked in recording2's save-state RAM: for all four bikes
#   *(bike+0x18) == *(chassis+0x254), and that transform's rows (at +0x80..+0xb0) equal the rows of
#   the chassis quaternion at +0x1a0. Keys there: RollGain 5.0, MaxRollCommand 0.7854,
#   MaxRollSpeed 51.333, MinStandUprightSpeed 14.667, MaxStandUprightSpeed 44.0.
# The two brake words the two halves read are not copies of one another: the bike's setup at
# 0x002535a0 hands the front wheel a pointer to bike+0x650 as its +0x30 and the rear wheel one to
# bike+0x654, beside the engine's +0x658/+0x65c (the rate-limited throttle and clutch), and each wheel
# reads its own word as a brake — 0x0025c4c8 in wheel_surface_drag turns it into a torque against the
# spin, 0x0025cb8c in wheel_tire_forces gates the locked-wheel path on it being nonzero. So the
# wheelie's veto is the driven wheel's brake and the stoppie's gate is the front's. Who writes the two
# words (one lever into two rate limiters, or two levers) was not chased down; the port keeps them as
# two inputs, Step::front_brake and Step::rear_brake.
# rotate_about_axis 0x001344e0 (the S7 block has it: angle in f12, unit axis, v, out) is the
# no-hander's last arithmetic — the ground normal turned about the node's own forward axis by minus
# the angle. Ported and proved by S7 as camera::rotate.
# The .phy keys the four controllers read, on top of S3's table. The loader is bike_phy_load
# 0x002539e0; a default in brackets is the one the loader passes when the key is absent.
#   DonutRollAngleScale       +0x540  [1.0]  (loaded at 0x00255380, far down the same function)
#   MaxRollCommand            +0x6d8  * 0.01745329 (degrees)
#   MaxRollSpeed              +0x6e0  * 1.46666
#   RollGain                  +0x6e8
#   MinStandUprightSpeed      +0x6ec  [10.0] * 1.46666
#   MaxStandUprightSpeed      +0x6f0  [30.0] * 1.46666
#   RollControlGain           +0x704
#   RollControlRateGain       +0x708
#   RollControlMaxCG          +0x70c
#   MaxRollRateDisturbance    +0x710
#   MaxPitchCommand           +0x714  * 0.01745329
#   PitchGain                 +0x718
#   PitchRateGain             +0x71c
#   WheelieTopSpeed           +0x720  stored as 1.0 / (key * 1.46666)
#   MaxStoppiePitchCommand    +0x730  * 0.01745329
#   StoppiePitchGain          +0x734
#   StoppieYawRateScale       +0x738
#   StoppieTopSpeed           +0x73c  * 1.46666
#   BalanceInertiaRate        +0xa50
#   MinBalanceInertia         +0xa54
#   MaxBalanceInertia         +0xa58
#   InputInertia              +0xa5c
#   BalanceNoiseAmplitude     +0xa68
#   BalanceNoiseTimeScale     +0xa70
#   MaxBalancePoint           +0xa74
#   StoppieBrake              +0x72c  the loader writes it and the pitch controller never reads it:
#                                 the stoppie latch at 0x002566a0 compares
#                                 the front brake (+0x650) against a zero built with `mtc1 $zero`,
#                                 and nothing in 0x00256470..0x00256940 touches +0x72c. pad_to_gesture
#                                 is what reads it. So it is a loader key and an input key, not one
#                                 of the ground step's twenty-five (S21)
# --- P2: the shell on the ported physics ----------------------------------------------------------
# No new function here — P2 wires the ported ones together — but two things had to be read off the
# disassembly to wire them, and both are easy to get backwards.
#
# chassis_integrate's two transform calls are in this order (./kit dis 0x0015cad0 --len 24, then
# 0x0015cb10 --len 20):
#   0x0015cadc  transform_rotate  of chassis+0x190 (the centre of gravity) against the transform as
#                                 it stood when integrate was entered
#   0x0015cb1c  orientation_set   writes the new rotation back
# So the origin the transform ends up at is built from the *previous* rotation, and the two calls
# after the set are answered against the new one. A port that answers all four against one matrix is
# wrong by one frame of rotation in the origin.
#
# engine_ctor (0x00246b70, in the S15 block above) is the only writer of two of the engine's words:
# +0x88, the clutch's launch multiplier, and +0xbc, the scale on the torque leaving the gearbox.
# Neither is a .phy key, so a port that builds its engine from the file alone leaves both at zero and
# the engine makes no torque at all: engine_update multiplies by +0x88 to get the torque and by +0xbc
# to get the torque reaching the wheel. Whoever constructs an engine has to apply the constructor's
# six ones, not just the loader's keys.
# --- P3: tricks in the shell ----------------------------------------------------------------------
# No new function either — P3 wires stunt_clocks, bike_wreck_test and the ride state machine onto the
# ported bike — but one field had been read two ways by two tasks and the disassembly settles it.
#
# bike +0x420..+0x42c is a *vector*, not four scalars: the bike constructor (0x0024f420, near its end)
# fills it from the constant quad at 0x00426db0, which is 0, 1, 0, 1 — the up axis — just as it fills
# +0x410..+0x41c from 0x00426dc0, which is 0, 0, 1, 1, the forward axis. So the no-hander clause at
# the bottom of bike_wreck_test,
#
#     if (*(int *)(obj + 0xa44) != 0 && *(float *)(obj + 0x424) < *(float *)(obj + 0x780))  -> reason 12
#
# is comparing the *y component of the bike's up axis* against +0x780, and +0x780 is where the .phy
# loader puts NoHanderMaxRollAngle *after* passing it through the game's cosine table. It is a roll
# test, not a speed test. NoHanderWreckSpeedLimit is +0x77c and is spent one clause higher up, against
# the vertical g load at +0x554 with the same +0xa44 flag in front of it. S11's journal reads +0x424
# as the bike's speed and +0x780 as the speed limit; that is wrong on both halves, and S10's air.rs is
# the correct port of the clause. Nothing calls trick.rs's version any more.
#
# The two reason codes that look like a clash are not one: bike_wreck_test's no-hander reason is 0xc
# and the trick machine's mid-trick reason is 12. Same number, same clause, two names.
# --- S23: the camera's two probes, and what its distance floor really is ---------------------------
# S7 read camera_update's distance floor as "whatever the camera's collision probe says". It is not:
# the argument is the *viewport's near clip plane*.
#
viewport_set_near           boot 0x00112a80 (near, viewport): writes viewport+0x19c and calls the
#                                           viewport's +0xc0. Three call sites, all in FUN_0020aaa0
#                                           (0x0020ac2c, 0x0020ae08, 0x0020aff0), each guarded by a
#                                           `... + 4 == 5` test on the chain at DAT_0041f770+0x3d4
#                                           and each passing 0x4019999a = 2.4
viewport_set_near_far       boot 0x001131d0 (near, far, viewport): writes +0x19c and +0x1a0 and builds
#                                           1/(1 - near/far) into the projection — a depth range, so
#                                           +0x19c is the near plane and nothing to do with collision
viewport_ctor               boot 0x00112310 puts 1.0 at +0x19c and 2.0 at +0x1a0
#
# The camera the game creates for a race is *not* the class S7 ported. It derives from it:
#
race_camera_ctor            boot 0x0021e1d0 called from 0x001ff270; vtable 0x00452c20
race_camera_vtable          boot 0x00452c20 overrides the base 0x00452060 at +0x008, +0x038, +0x07c,
#                                           +0x094, +0x0dc, +0x108 (-> 0x0021e4d0), +0x10c (->
#                                           0x0021e7d0) and +0x12c
race_camera_update          boot 0x0021e880 its frame. Distance floor = viewport+0x19c * 4.5, then the
#                                           same min/max against the preset, so on the disc the floor
#                                           is max(MinFollowingDistance, 10.8). No push-out block at
#                                           all. Its look-ahead differs from the base's: speed as
#                                           handed instead of the velocity along the heading and no
#                                           cap at 1.0, one fall correction (vel.y < -9.0, / 1.8)
#                                           instead of two at +-5.0, the look-ahead clamped at zero,
#                                           and a look-back preset handled by a zero look-ahead
#                                           instead of an early return down the reversed heading
race_camera_ground_probe    boot 0x0021e7d0 vtable slot 0x10c, the override of the base's
#                                           `return 0.0f`: ground_query (0x001dc960) on a copy of the
#                                           point, then `if (point.y < snapped.y) lift = (snapped.y -
#                                           point.y) * 0.6 else lift = lift * 0.02`, where lift is the
#                                           camera's own +0x18c — last frame's answer. Ported as
#                                           camera::Follow::probe_ground
race_camera_look_at         boot 0x0021f320 builds the probe's point as swung_heading * ((|pitch| /
#                                           1.5707964) * 1.2 + 0.3) + target_point, on the same
#                                           normalised swung heading the look-ahead point rides, and
#                                           writes y = y_term + height_bias + lift. The base
#                                           (0x001fb600) builds the same point and writes y = y_term +
#                                           lift + height_bias — the one float order the two classes
#                                           disagree on
race_camera_position        boot 0x0021f600 the derived position; not read
#
# The base class's own probe, which is the only part of the camera that asks where anything solid is:
# camera_update's last act, gated on cam+0xe8 (the collision object, zeroed by the ctor 0x001f9910
# and written by camera_make_probe below, which is a vtable slot and not the ctor — which is why a
# reader of the frame alone finds no writer). Segment from target_point + rider_up * 2.0 to the
# filtered position, both quads keeping the w they were built from; on a hit,
# position = cam+0xb0 - normalize(end - start) * 2.0, normalised through the EE FPU (mula.s/madda.s/
# madd.s then rsqrt.s, not VU0's approximate rsqrt), a zero-length segment leaving the direction at
# the constant quad 0x00426d70 = (0, 0, 0, 1). The lags are not told. Ported as camera::push_out.
probe_bundle_set_points     boot 0x001e8850 (obj, count, points or null): 2*count points into +0x128
#                                           and a float each into +0x124, zeroed when null
probe_object_moved          boot 0x00119be0 the "this object moved" refresh
probe_collide_world         boot 0x0011a150 the generic collide-against-the-world routine
#
# Where the collision object at cam+0xe8 comes from, which no reader of the frame alone can see: the
# vtable slot the race's setup calls once, not the constructor.
camera_make_probe           boot 0x001fa100 (cam, point, world) base vtable slot 0xdc: allocates a
#                                           0x170-byte probe object into cam+0xe8, gives it one probe
#                                           (0x001e8460), the camera as its owner at +0x78 and the
#                                           hit callback at +0x10c, then sets its points. The derived
#                                           class's own is 0x0021e350, the same but for its callback
#                                           and its __FILE__ string
camera_probe_hit            boot 0x001f98d0 (probe object) that callback, a bare label: copies
#                                           record+0x98 into cam+0xb0 (the hit point) and record+0x20
#                                           into cam+0xc0 (the nearest normal), and sets the flag at
#                                           cam+0x170 that the frame reads one line later
race_camera_create          boot 0x001ff270 builds the race camera and hands it the world: the write
#                                           of the world pointer into camera+0x184 that
#                                           race_camera_ground_probe reads. Also the ctor's one caller
#
# The field of view, for the other half of S23: the angle in cpresets.ini is the *horizontal* one and
# the vertical one follows the shape of the viewport, so a port that hard-codes 4:3 is wrong on every
# other window. The split-screen layouts are the proof that the engine reads its real shape.
viewport_set_window         boot 0x00112670 (viewport, x, y, w, h) -> +0x180..+0x18c and the shape
#                                           term +0x198 = (h/w) / (+0x17c * +0x1a8/+0x1a4), the
#                                           second factor being the pixel aspect of a PS2 display
#                                           mode, which a square-pixel window makes 1
viewport_build_projection   boot 0x00112e00 (viewport) -> frustum_from_fov(+0x19c, +0x1a0, +0x170 *
#                                           0.01745329, 1.0 / +0x198) into the viewport's own 4x4
frustum_from_fov            boot 0x0013e170 (near, far, horizontal fov in radians, aspect, out 4x4):
#                                           a wrapper that copies the 4x4 out
frustum_half_angles         boot 0x001e7840 (fov in f12, aspect in f13, near in f14, far, out, 0):
#                                           half width = near * tan(fov/2), the tangent read out of
#                                           the game's own {cos, sin} curve table with a first-order
#                                           correction, and half height = that / aspect, into
#                                           0x001e7700 as (left, right, bottom, top, near, far)
# --- S22: the g loads the wreck test reads, and where a wheel's geometry comes from ---------------
# The symptom was a standing bike wrecking itself: P3 measured the port's sideways g load alternating
# sign and growing frame over frame off the start line, past SideWreckSpeedLimit in about 2.5 s. The
# g loads were not ringing. They were reading the wrong field.
#
# bike_ground_step clears **chassis+0x170** (and +0x180) in the same breath as the bike's own
# +0x550..+0x55c, once a frame, and the line that feeds the g loads is
#
#     transform_unrotate(&local, bike+0x18, chassis+0x170);   // then max by magnitude into +0x550
#
# once per substep, just before the integrate. chassis+0x170 is **not** the body's acceleration at
# +0x70 that chassis_integrate writes from the force: it is an *impact* accumulator, and the only
# thing in the frame that writes it is the bottom-out branch of wheel_tire_forces, which takes the
# into-the-ground part out of chassis+0x260 and subtracts the same scalar, along the body's own up
# brought into the world (the constant (0,1,0,1) at 0x00426db0), from chassis+0x170. No dt: what the
# accumulator holds is the closing speed the shove cancelled, not that over a step. A ride that never
# bottoms out therefore holds g loads of exactly zero. 300 frames of the original idling off the line
# (watch/s22-standing.toml) read +0x550, +0x170 and +0x180 as zero on every row while +0x70 is
# nonzero throughout, which is the whole proof in one capture. The port had the accumulator (it is
# mv::Chassis::accel and wheel::Chassis::accel, both +0x170) and the shell was syncing it with the
# body's +0x70 around the integrate and unrotating it once a frame instead of once a substep.
#
# bike+0x18 is the same transform as the chassis' +0x254: bike_attach_nodes below hands it to the
# chassis through the body's vtable +0x74. So "unrotate by the bike's transform" and "unrotate by the
# chassis' transform" are one thing, and the port's separate bike node (which keeps the no-hander's
# roll) is not involved.
bike_attach_nodes boot 0x00253760 (bike) hangs the physics off the bike's model: the chassis is given
#                                bike+0x18 as its transform (vtable +0x74), then the node named
#                                "TireFront" is looked up in it (0x001b37b0 by name, 0x001b2870 for
#                                its place in that frame) and written straight into the front wheel's
#                                +0xa0, "Shocks" and "Handlebars" become the normalised steering axis
#                                at front wheel +0x8c0, and "TireRear" lands at the rear wheel's
#                                +0xa0. So a wheel's mount is the *model's* own tire node and not a
#                                .phy key: the live wheels read (0, 1.2332027, 2.5224998) and
#                                (0, 1.1946994, -2.5897298) on a cr125_faisst, a 5.11 ft wheelbase,
#                                which is exactly what the bike's TireFront/TireRear nodes measure.
#                                BodyTireForcePosition is a different field: bike_phy_load puts it at
#                                the wheel's +0xb0 (./kit dis 0x00253d3c --len 32) and neither
#                                wheel_suspension nor wheel_tire_forces ever reads it -- both of the
#                                +0xb0 loads in those two are the *transform's* +0xb0, its translation
wheel_contact_velocity boot 0x0025b710 (wheel) the contact-velocity step, wheel+0x170 and the
#                                     chassis' own at +0x1a0..: the arm is **wheel+0x140 (the load
#                                     point) minus chassis+0x60 (the centre of gravity in the world)**,
#                                     crossed with chassis+0x280 and added to chassis+0x260. Not the
#                                     contact point at +0x120, which is what this port measures it at
#
# wheel_set_suspension's arithmetic in full, which the loader calls twice (SpringWhenStopped, then
# Spring) and the ground step overwrites +0x54 of every frame:
#   +0x48 = StrutCompression          +0x4c = MaxCompressionRate   +0x50 = MaxDecompressionRate
#   +0x54 = |spring * mass * 32.2|    (mass is chassis+0x210)
#   +0x58 = BounceDampingRatio  * 2 * sqrt(mass * (+0x54))
#   +0x5c = ReboundDampingRatio * 2 * sqrt(mass * (+0x54))
# The damping ratios are of *critical* damping, against the rate the setter has just worked out from
# `Spring` -- not ratios of the spring rate itself, which is 3.5x too stiff on a cr125_faisst's front
# and 4.5x on its rear. Live: rate 439.999939 (the stopped rate, re-blended per frame), damping
# 51.7998924 front and 89.164505 rear, which is 2*0.3*sqrt(12.42236*600) and
# 2*0.4*sqrt(12.42236*1000) to the bit.
#
# And the probe: wheel+0x214 is **one tire radius** (live 1.19999993 = TireRadius), not
# StrutCompression. wheel_set_tire_derived builds the suspension line from it and wheel_suspension
# re-aims it by -(+0x214) every step of its relaxation; StrutCompression is +0x48, how far the strut
# may compress before it bottoms out, and that is all it is.

# --- S24: the rest of the race camera's frame, which is the one the picture comes from -------------
# S23 found the derived class and three of its differences. The whole frame reads as follows, and is
# ported as camera::Race. Addresses already above: race_camera_update 0x0021e880 (the frame),
# race_camera_look_at 0x0021f320, race_camera_position 0x0021f600, race_camera_ground_probe 0x0021e7d0.
#
race_camera_aim             boot 0x0021efc0 (swing, speed, forward.y in f12..f14, cam). Not in the
#                                           derived vtable at all — race_camera_update is its one
#                                           caller, where the base reaches camera_aim through slot
#                                           0x118. The base's yaw exactly; the pitch without the
#                                           reverse ramp, and with two
#                                           trims after the +-1.4835324 clamp. First: below
#                                           LowSpeedAngleXVelCutoff (and only when that is not zero)
#                                           pitch *= k + (speed/cutoff)*(1-k) and then pitch -= k *
#                                           |forward.y|, k = LowSpeedAngleXScaleFactor. That is the
#                                           only reader found for either field; S7 recorded both as
#                                           unread. Second: pitch -= 0.33 * AngleX when the mode word
#                                           at 0x004211a0 is not 3, *(*(app_pointer+0x3cc)+0x78) is 0,
#                                           and (u32 viewport+0x18c)/(u32 viewport+0x188) < 0.55 —
#                                           a viewport less than 0.55 as tall as it is wide, i.e. a
#                                           horizontally split screen
camera_no_look_ahead_bit    boot 0x0041f770 bit 3 (0x08) of the byte at *(app_pointer) + 0x325, which
#                                           the frame tests before the speed: set, the look-at is the
#                                           plain target point + height bias and the ground probe is
#                                           not asked at all, and the position takes the standing-
#                                           still lead. Cleared by mode_leave_world (0x00202da0) and
#                                           written from a parameter by FUN_00207770 (46 callers);
#                                           what the engine means by it was not pinned down
race_camera_split_mode      boot 0x004211a0 the word race_camera_aim's second trim tests against 3
#
# The frame, in order, with $17 = cam and $f24 = dt (frame size 480):
#   1. no actor at cam+0x8c -> camera_base_update(dt, cam) and return
#   2. fov = vtable+0x110 (camera_get_fov, not overridden)
#   3. target point = actor+0x10 + forward*offset.z + up*offset.y (+ cross(up,forward)*offset.x only
#      when offset.x != 0) — the base's, unchanged
#   4. heading = normalize(xz of (actor+0xb0 != 0 && actor+0xb4 > 20.0 ? actor+0x50 : actor+0x30));
#      y zeroed with an integer store, w from the source row
#   5. speed = actor+0xb4, *read off the actor*, where the base takes |velocity| every frame;
#      airborne = actor+0xb0 (S24 wrote "grounded"; it is on_ground == 0, P34)
#   6. swing = (0.21 * -lean) * (fov / MaxFOV). No CameraSwing factor — the base has one here. The aim
#      still multiplies the yaw by CameraSwing, so the preset is not ignored, only this share of it
#   7. reverse: no acos, no ramps. if (speed > 4.0 && !IsLookBackCam) and heading.vel < 0 then the
#      local velocity becomes the constant quad 0x00426d70 = (0,0,0,1) and speed becomes 0. The three
#      reverse ramps are still read (by the distance and nothing else here) and never written
#   8. race_camera_aim(swing, speed, actor+0x34, cam) — the rider's own forward.y, not the heading's
#   9. direction basis = normalize(xz of ((airborne && speed > 5.0 && |speed - |vel.y|| > 0.01) ?
#      vel : heading)) with w = 1.0 written as a literal
#  10. sideways = cross(basis, UP); dir = rotate(wrap(yaw), UP, rotate(wrap(pitch), sideways, basis)) —
#      both angles corrected into -pi..pi first, which the base's frame does not do
#  11. distance: floor = max(MinFollowingDistance, 4.5 * viewport+0x19c), then the base's clamp
#  12. still branch (camera_no_look_ahead_bit set, or speed < 0.0001): look-at = target point with
#      y += height_bias, and the position's target is the plain target point. Moving branch: the
#      position's target is target + vel * min(dt, 0.2) and the look-at is race_camera_look_at of it
#  13. race_camera_position(dt, -lean, speed, out, cam, dir, that target, heading, &look_at, sideways,
#      actor+0x50) — the actor's *raw* velocity, not the copy step 7 may have zeroed. Of its eleven
#      arguments it reads five: out, cam, dir, target, speed, vel. The heading, the look-at and the
#      sideways axis are dead
#  14. spare = 0; vtable+0x124 (camera_filter, not overridden) with the *plain* target point
#  15. viewport_set_eye(viewport, &position, 0, 0, 0, &fov); viewport+0x26c -> +0xc4(viewport,
#      &look_at, UP); camera_base_update(dt, cam)
#  16. no push-out block at all, and no test of cam+0xe8
#
# race_camera_look_at 0x0021f320 (speed, fov ratio, swing in f12..f14; out, cam, target, heading,
# basis, &vel, reverse) in full. The heading and the reverse flag are dead — the swung vector is the
# *basis*, not the heading, and it is not normalised afterwards (no rsqrt in the routine; the base
# normalises the same rotation):
#   k    = (distance * fov ratio) / 90.0 + 1.0      (written 1.0 + x)
#   lead = (speed / SpeedReference) * (k * ((1.8 * (1.0 - distance/600.0)) * k))
#   if (vel.y < -9.0) lead *= 1.0 + (vel.y / speed) / 1.8
#   ahead = IsLookBackCam ? 0.0 : max(0.0, lead * LookAheadDistance)
#   swung = rotate(wrap(swing * (-0.7 * LookSwing)), UP, basis)
#   point = target + swung * ahead                  (w from the target)
#   y     = point.y + (1.0 - fov ratio) * (0.25 * vel.y)   (the base associates this the other way)
#   lift  = vtable+0x10c(cam, swung * ((|pitch|/1.5707964) * 1.2 + 0.3) + target)
#   out   = (point.x, y + (height_bias + lift), point.z, point.w)
# The look-back cam runs all of that with a zero lead, where the base returns early down the reversed
# heading and skips the ground probe with it.
#
# race_camera_position 0x0021f600 in full: out = target + dir * distance, and then, if speed < 0.0001,
# + (vel * dt) * 10.0. No dead yaw swing in it; the base's is dead code.
#
race_camera_frame_setup     boot 0x0021e4d0 vtable slot 0x108, the override of camera_frame_noop's
#                                           `return 1`. S24 read it as unreached; it is not: the base
#                                           filter calls slot 0x108 on the position before and after
#                                           the lags. It is the eye's floor — see S103 below. Ported
#                                           as camera::Race::keep_off_the_ground
#
# --- S103: what keeps the race camera's eye out of the ground --------------------------------------
# camera_filter (unoverridden) opens with slot0x108(cam, position, target, 1, 1) on the raw position
# and, in the arm that runs the lags, ends with slot0x108(cam, &lagged, target, 1, 0) on the lagged
# position (w 1.0) before writing it out. The lags keep the unlifted lagged value. In the base class
# the slot is camera_frame_noop; in the race camera it is race_camera_frame_setup, which is (args cam,
# &eye, target, lift, lock):
#   floor = max.s(viewport+0x19c * 2.0, cam+0x1a0)
#   if cam+0xe8 (collision object): segment probe from target (or last frame's start +0x190 after a
#     hit) less its unit direction, stretched to 2*floor when shorter than floor, through the
#     object's moved/collide (0x00119be0, 0x0011a9d0, with the +0xec filter 0x00218b80); a hit
#     (callback 0x0021e190, byte-identical to camera_probe_hit) puts eye = hit + normal * floor
#   if cam+0x184 (world): ground_query on the eye as handed in; y = min.s(eye.y, cam+0x1a4);
#     if (y - ground < floor) { if lock: cam+0x16c = 1; if lift: eye.y = ground + floor }
#     else if lock: cam+0x16c = 0.                                     returns 1 (0: under, no lift)
# cam+0x16c is the flag camera_nudge tests before letting the stick pitch down (Follow's
# pitch_down_locked). The derived nudge 0x0021f770 does not read it.
race_camera_setup           boot 0x0021e270 (f12 0.55, f13 ceiling, f14 0.6, f15 2.0, cam, ...) from
#                                           race_camera_create: cam+0x1a0 = f15 (2.0, over the ctor's
#                                           3.0), cam+0x1a4 = f13 = 200.0 when the scene's fog record
#                                           +0x1c (MaximumFogDistance) > 1500, else FLT_MAX
race_camera_probe_hit       boot 0x0021e190 the derived make-probe 0x0021e350's hit callback
# Live, three states (first_recording, recording2, biplane), both race camera objects in each:
# +0xe8 = 0 and +0xec = 0 (the collision object is never made — slot 0xdc, which makes it, has no
# caller in the race's setup), +0x184 = the world, +0x1a0 = 2.0, +0x1a4 = FLT_MAX, viewport near 2.4.
# So on the disc the floor is 4.8 ft over the ground under the eye and the segment probe never runs.
# free09's fog record +0x1c reads 1000.0, the biplane state's 768.0.

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# S25 — the vehicle's own animation state machine, which is the only live caller of what S20 ported:
# a sixty-entry jump table on the bike's animation state, its own clip table, the sixth arm of the
# library dispatch, and the two arms that ask for the four-clip blend. The port is
# crates/game-sim/src/vehicle_anim.rs, the proofs are crates/game-sim/tests/vehicle_anim.rs, and how
# each one was found is in research/journal/2026-10-16-s25-vehicle-animation.
#
# This supersedes the S20 block's note on vehicle_animation above: it *is* ported now, all of it, and
# Ghidra's "it drops both of those blocks" is why the whole function was read through `./kit dis`
# (0x002492b0..0x00249ca4, 637 instructions) rather than the decompiler. Two things in that note to
# correct: the four-clip arms pick their pairs out of +0x960..+0x980 of a table that starts at +0x900
# and is indexed by state, not out of a table of its own; and the mode register holds 0, 1, 2, 4 and 5
# — never 3, in this copy of the dispatch either.
#
# The vehicle is its own motion object: anim_bind_clip, anim_clip_handle, motion_play, anim_rewind and
# all six library entries take the vehicle pointer itself, so +0x40/+0x44 (the bound handles) and
# +0x48..+0x94 (the clock) are the same fields the S20 block describes.
#
# The vehicle's own fields, by offset. A test may say where these live; the port may not:
#   +0x30   the node the placement writes          +0x1e0  the name buffer the loader fills first
#   +0x2ac  }  both non-zero and a request that is not holding skips the placement entirely
#   +0x2b0  }
#   +0x488  a pointer to the request block below   +0x630/+0x634  pointers to the two lag records
#   +0x680  "this frame came off the clip table"   +0x684  holding a pose   +0x688  ever started
#   +0x68c  the cross-fade ramp                    +0x690  the rate it falls at, picked per restart
#   +0x694  ready rate  +0x698  hold rate  +0x69c  default rate  +0x6a0  pose rate  +0x6a4  stunt rate
#   +0x8dc  the new clip's phase  +0x8e0  the old clip's  +0x8e4  where the old one started
#   +0x8e8  the longest the stunt cross-fade may take
#   +0x8ec  the previous state  +0x8f0  the state running  +0x8f4  the state asked for
#   +0x8f8  the phase the stunt cross-fade starts the old clip at
#   +0x8fc  a *byte*: the request count the restart test compares against. Nothing here writes it
#   +0x900..+0x9ec  the sixty-entry clip table, indexed by animation state
#   +0x9f0  which added record to play instead, or negative for none
#   +0x9f4  the added-record list: its count, +0x9f8 a flag byte, +0x9fc the array of pointers
#   +0xa14/+0xa18/+0xa1c  the pose grid's three thresholds   +0xa3c  the reverse pair's phase clock
# The request block (the pointer at +0x488): +0x8 take the position from the base, +0xc holding,
# +0xf0 a pointer to the offset vector, +0x140 cut the start-line group short, +0x170 the phase axis'
# target, +0x174 the mix axis', +0x178 the number the pose grid cuts up, +0x180 the base position
# (quadword), +0x190 the rotation quaternion, +0x1a4 how far through the gesture, +0x1a8 the request
# count.
# A lag record is three floats: {value, time constant, the ratio the last step left}.
#
# The frame, in the order it runs: the placement (transform_set_position then
# transform_set_rotation); the restart test, which is `asked != running || (seq >= 2 && seq != the
# byte at +0x8fc)`; the fade-rate switch, which only runs on a restart; the restart itself
# (anim_rewind, +0x688 = 1, +0x68c = 1.0, all three weights zeroed); the held request's override
# (+0x690 and +0x68c zeroed); the latch of +0x8f4 into +0x8f0; `+0x68c -= +0x690 * dt`; +0x680
# cleared; the hold released when the ramp has run out, on every state but 0x26; the two axes, snapped
# first if the request is holding and the target is not FLT_MAX, then stepped — the one at +0x630
# before the one at +0x634; the selection; and then the three weights and the ramp clamped to 0..1 and
# the dispatch.
#
# The fade-rate table is at 0x00431a80, indexed by the state *asked for* (`sltiu` against 60 in front
# of it, so a negative one is out of range and takes the default):
#   0x00..0x17, 0x27                     -> +0x6a4   the gesture slots and nohandedlander
#   0x18..0x20, 0x24, 0x25, out of range -> +0x69c   the default
#   0x21, 0x22                           -> +0x69c   and +0xa3c = 0: the reverse pair's clock
#   0x23, 0x26                           -> +0x6a0   the airborne row and the backflip
#   0x28..0x2d                           -> +0x698   the wreck-and-hold group
#   0x2e..0x38                           -> 0 if the request asks to cut, else +0x694
#   0x39..0x3b                           -> +0x694   the three start poses, which cannot be cut
# The state table is at 0x00431990, indexed by the state *running*, same range test:
#   0..23  -> 0x249a24  the gesture slots: the pair at two phases, or the new clip alone
#   30     -> 0x2496f8  the pose grid, which is where the four-clip blend's two callers are
#   33, 34 -> 0x249978  the reverse pair, at its own clock
#   35     -> 0x249928  the airborne row
#   38     -> 0x2499e0  the backflip, held at a tenth of its clip
#   39     -> 0x249a04  nohandedlander
#   40..59 -> 0x2496cc  the two played-once groups: motion_play on the frame they start
#   24..29, 31, 32, 36, 37 -> 0x249b14  no arm at all, which is the free-running advance
# The two writers of mode 4 are 0x002497b4 (the band below the first threshold, mixing the stopped row
# with the still row) and 0x002498d4 (the band between the second and third, mixing the still row with
# the riding row). Each is the delay slot of the branch out of its own arm, which is why the two
# `addiu $17, 4` sit after an unconditional branch and Ghidra drops both blocks.
# The rows are four states apart in groups of three: 24..26 stopped, 27..29 still, 30..32 riding,
# 33/34 the reverse pair, 35..37 airborne. The within-row mix is the mix axis stretched over each half
# (`mix < 0.5 ? 2*mix : 2*(mix-0.5)`) and it picks which two of the three the pair is.
# The function itself is `vehicle_animation` (boot 0x002492b0, 2548 bytes, dt in f12 and the vehicle
# in a0), named in the S20 block above and resolved under that name by this task's proofs.
transform_set_position boot 0x001b2660 (transform in a0, space in a1, position in a2) write a node's
#                                     position. a1 == 0 and no parent (+0x100) is a straight store of
#                                     the quadword through 0x001b2640; a1 == a0 adds it to +0x70
#                                     instead; anything else brings it through the parent. The
#                                     placement passes 0, so the vector in a2 is what the step decided
motion_blend_pair boot 0x00147640 (the first clip's phase in f12, the second clip's in f13, the mix in
#                                 f14, the cross-fade in f15, motion in a0, clips in a1/a2, five
#                                 zeros) the sixth entry point, and 0x00249c58 is its only caller in
#                                 the image. The one entry that samples its two clips at two
#                                 *different* times: param_1 = a.duration * f12 and param_2 =
#                                 b.duration * f13, where motion_blend_two would have used one time in
#                                 seconds for both. The clock is driven off a alone, exactly as the
#                                 other entries drive it off their first clip -- +0x48 cleared, +0x50
#                                 = a's handle, +0x54 = a's time, motion_events over the span -- and
#                                 it has no test of that time against zero, so a clip it has only just
#                                 been handed fires the empty window. The mix goes to
#                                 motion_slerp(f14, out = b, from = b, to = a, 1), so 0 is all of b
#                                 and 1 is all of a. It writes no pose fields of its own
vehicle_load_clips boot 0x002483a0 (vehicle in a0, the first stunt record in a1, the name prefix in
#                                  a2) the vehicle's clip table. Two passes over 24 records of stride
#                                  0x54, each building "<prefix><Name>" with snprintf into a 32-byte
#                                  buffer -- the first calls anim_find_or_load and anim_bind_clip for
#                                  *every* record, with no UsedForBike gate, which is the one place it
#                                  differs from rider_load_clips; the second writes
#                                  anim_clip_handle(anim_clip_by_name(...)) into +0x900 + i*4. Then 36
#                                  literal names, bare -- no "bike_" -- looked up but never loaded,
#                                  into +0x960..+0x9ec. The strings are at 0x431608 ("%s%s") and
#                                  0x431658..0x431950. It opens by writing its own name buffer at
#                                  +0x1e0 from two globals (0x004e1eb0 and 0x0041e930), which nothing
#                                  in the animation reads
vehicle_extra_at boot 0x0024eee0 (list in a0, index in a1) one element of the added-record list:
#                                `*(*(list+8) + index*4)`, five instructions and no range check, so an
#                                index past the count reads whatever is behind the array. A record is
#                                12 bytes: {the key the caller recognises, the clip the mix goes
#                                towards, the clip it comes from}
vehicle_add_anim boot 0x00248150 (vehicle in a0, key in a1, the first clip's name in a2, the second's
#                                in a3) append one added record, if the flag byte at +0x9f8 is set and
#                                no record already has that key. Each name is loaded, bound and looked
#                                up the same way the loader does it; an empty name leaves that half of
#                                the record 0. Not ported: it is the caller's half, and what the
#                                machine does with a record is proved from records laid out in RAM

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# S27 — the rider's own animation state machine: the function in *front* of vehicle_animation, which
# is what decides the state that step is handed. The port is crates/game-sim/src/rider_anim.rs, the
# proofs are crates/game-sim/tests/rider_anim.rs, and how it was found is in
# research/journal/2026-10-17-s27-rider-animation.
#
# This corrects the S25 block above on one point of naming, and answers the question its journal left
# open. The actor S25 calls "the vehicle" is the **rider's body**: the spawn that allocates it
# (boot 0x0020e4f0) hands it the prefix string "rider_" at 0x0042ead8 or "ff_rider_" at 0x0042eb10 --
# the two modes the disc's rider archive spells [Stunts] and [ff_Stunts] in its motions.ini -- and
# vehicle_load_clips joins that prefix to the 24 gesture names only, looking the other 36 up bare.
# Those 36 bare names are a rider's own .mcf clips (V8 proved all 24 gesture slots and the 3 airborne
# poses bind under the rider's rule), so the sixty-entry table is the *rider's* clip table and
# vehicle_anim::POSE_CLIPS is a list of rider clips. The state table's addresses, offsets and arms in
# the S25 block are all still right; only the word "vehicle" in it is wrong.
#
# The open question of the S25 journal -- who writes the byte at +0x8fc that the restart test compares
# the request's count against -- is answered by this function's tail: it writes both +0x8f8 (which S25
# read as the configured start phase, and which is in fact *last frame's* progress through the
# gesture) and the byte at +0x8fc, from the request's +0x1a4 and +0x1a8, after the step returns. So on
# a live body the count moves only when the caller moves it, and a repeat of the running state
# restarts on that frame alone.
#
# The body's own fields, by offset, on top of the S25 map. A test may say where these live; the port
# may not:
#   +0x3c   the motion object's "the clip has run out", which the look arm clears
#   +0x6b4  the wrecked body has come to rest. Written by the body's mode machine (boot 0x0024c7e0,
#           3592 bytes, which also drives the head and look dummies); read here and nowhere else
#   +0xa0c  the gate has drawn its idle          +0xa28  what is left of the gate's breathe-and-wait
#   +0xa2c  a look-around idle is playing        +0xa30  what is left of the wait before the next one
#   +0xa34  the shortest wait (15.0 as built)    +0xa38  the longest (45.0)
#   +0xa10  slower than this, either way, and the body counts as standing still (1.4666667 as built)
#   +0xa14  the pose grid's first threshold (7.3333 as built), which doubles as the speed that cancels
#           the look timer -- the one field this function and the step read for different reasons
# The request block's other half (the pointer at +0x488; S25's block has the fields the step reads):
#   +0xf8  airborne   +0xfc  a backflip is in progress   +0x100 the gesture is a no-hander
#   +0x104 the body is off the bike              +0x144 the gate's own clock, in seconds
#   +0x160 the throttle                         +0x16c  going backwards
#   +0x1a0 the gesture slot asked for, or 24 for none
# V35 — what fills three of these, from a capture (research/journal/2026-10-24-v35-backflip-animation,
#   context/capture/v35/flips.csv, recording2 2374..3473, 1100 rows): +0xfc == the bike's flipping
#   (bike_backflip's +0x4f8, set with +0x4f4 the substep the torque goes in) on 1100 of 1100 rows, the
#   same tick; +0xf8 == the bike's +0x57c on every riding row; +0x1a0 == the trick id +0xa78 when below
#   24, else 24. The trick id is 25 (the airborne idle) all through a flip, never 24. State 38 runs
#   2513..2543 (flip 1, until a Taunt asked mid-flip outranks it); flip 2's trick is asked a tick before
#   it turns, so 38 never shows. The writer itself was still not located.
#
# The selection is a chain of else-ifs and the first arm that matches is the only one that runs: the
# gate (+0x140 set), the wreck (+0x104 set), a gesture (+0x1a0 != 24), and then the backflip, the
# airborne pose, the reverse pair and the idle-and-look-around. The states it can ask for are exactly
# the states the step's jump table has an arm for -- 0..23, 30, 33, 34, 35, 38..59 -- and never one of
# the ten (24..29, 31, 32, 36, 37) that fall to the step's free-running advance.
#
# Four arms draw a random number, each through the same lazily installed seed, and each `rand` call
# site is preceded by the same inlined test of rand_seeded below. The draws are
# `(int)(unit * n) + base`: 10 idles from 0x2f, 3 start poses from 0x39, 5 held limbs from 0x29, and
# one bare unit remapped onto +0xa34..+0xa38 for the look timer (through the engine's shared remap
# with constant bounds of 0.0 and 1.0, which is why Ghidra reports "Removing unreachable block
# (ram,0x0024923c)" -- that block is the remap's `in_lo == in_hi` guard).
rider_animation boot 0x00248a70 (dt in f12, body in a0) the rider's animation selection, 2068 bytes:
#                               the only caller of vehicle_animation, a slot of the body actor's
#                               vtable at 0x004544b8 and 0x00458f58, and the writer of +0x8f4 (the
#                               state asked for), +0x8f8 and +0x8fc
rider_idle_pool boot 0x004e1fc0 the ten get-ready idles' no-repeat list: ten words of state ids
rider_idle_count boot 0x004e1fe8 how many of rider_idle_pool are in use. 0 and 10 both start a new
#                                round of one; a draw already in the list is thrown away and redrawn
rand_seeded boot 0x0045fa10 the seed `rand` was last given, and the flag that says it has been given
#                           one: zero and the next draw anywhere in the program reads the CD drive's
#                           clock (boot 0x0041a068, a BCD sceCdCLOCK: second at +1, minute at +2, hour
#                           at +3, day at +5), stores `second + minute*60 + hour*0x168 + day*0x15180`
#                           here and hands it to srand. 0x168 is 360, not 3600, so the hour's
#                           contribution is a sixth of an hour's worth of seconds

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# NOTE (merge, not either task): this block and the S27 block above are two independent decodes of
# the same function, 0x00248a70, made in parallel. They agree on every fact, including the S25
# correction and the tail writer, and they name it twice: rider_animation / vehicle_anim_want,
# rider_idle_pool / vehicle_anim_start_idles, rand_seeded / vehicle_anim_rand_seeded. Both names
# resolve, because each task's tests ask for its own. vehicle_anim_ctor and vehicle_anim_build are
# V11's alone. The duplicate port is for a human to settle; see context/notes/master.md.
# V11 — the state chooser: the one writer of the sixty-state machine's `want`, and so the one thing
# between a ride and the four-clip blend. It is a method of the same object S25 mapped (which the
# disc says is the *rider*, not the bike: all 36 of the fixed clip names are in every rider archive
# and none are in 250sx.res). The port is `Update` in crates/game-sim/src/vehicle_anim.rs, the proof
# is the fourth test in crates/game-sim/tests/vehicle_anim.rs, and the decode is in
# research/journal/2026-10-17-v11-blends-that-never-run.
vehicle_anim_want boot 0x00248a70 (dt in f12, the object in a0) the chooser, 2068 bytes. Writes +0x8f4
#                                 and calls vehicle_animation (0x002492b0) in its own tail; after the
#                                 call it writes +0x8f8 = request+0x1a4 and the byte +0x8fc =
#                                 (char)request+0x1a8, which is the writer the S25 block above says
#                                 nothing has. Four parts: the start-line group off request+0x140;
#                                 the riding priority (wrecked, gesture, flipping, airborne,
#                                 reversing, else the grid); the idle wait; the tail. On its first
#                                 call it seeds the generator from gettimeofday unless the flag at
#                                 0x0045fa10 is set, so a proof sets that flag
vehicle_anim_rand_seeded boot 0x0045fa10 the once-only flag in front of that srand call
vehicle_anim_start_idles boot 0x004e1fc0 a file-static list of the start idles other bodies on the
#                                        line have already drawn, ten ints, count at 0x004e1fe8. The
#                                        chooser redraws until its pick is not in the list, appends
#                                        it, and resets the list to the one new entry once all ten
#                                        are gone. The port models it as `vehicle_anim::Shared`
#                                        because it is shared between bodies, not owned by one
# The object's fields the chooser owns, past the S25 map:
#   +0x3c   the motion object's "the clip finished" flag (read, and cleared when a fidget starts)
#   +0x6b4  "the wreck has come to rest", written by another method of the object (0x0024c7e0)
#   +0xa0c  a start pose has been drawn   +0xa10  the still band, 1.46667
#   +0xa28  the breathe timer   +0xa2c  a fidget is playing   +0xa30  the idle countdown
#   +0xa34/+0xa38  the countdown's range in seconds, 15 and 45
# The request block, past the S25 map: +0xf8 airborne, +0xfc flipping, +0x100 no hands, +0x104
# wrecked, +0x144 how long the body has been settled, +0x160 the commanded throttle, +0x16c
# reversing, +0x1a0 the gesture asked for (24 is "none"). The writer of the block is not located
vehicle_anim_ctor boot 0x002472c0 the object's constructor, 1396 bytes, which is where the numbers the
#                                 step and the chooser read come from. The five fade rates
#                                 (+0x694..+0x6a4) and the stunt cross-fade cap (+0x8e8) are *zero*
#                                 here and written by something not located; these are not:
#                                   +0xa10 1.46666  the still band
#                                   +0xa14 7.3333   }  the pose grid's three thresholds
#                                   +0xa18 14.6666  }
#                                   +0xa1c 21.9999  }
#                                   +0xa20 73.3330  }  two more the step does not read
#                                   +0xa24 80.6666  }
#                                   +0xa34 15.0  +0xa38 45.0  the idle countdown's range in seconds
#                                   +0x8f4 = +0x8f0 = +0x8ec = 0x1e: the body starts in the pose grid
#                                 1/1.46666 is 0.681818, which is the number openmx/src/main.rs turns
#                                 the chassis speed into mph with — so the band is feet per second and
#                                 the six thresholds are 1, 5, 10, 15, 50 and 55 mph exactly
vehicle_anim_build boot 0x00247a60 (728 bytes) the body's build, which makes the two lag records the
#                                  axes are eased in: five words each, {value 0.5, time constant,
#                                  1.0, FLT_MAX, -FLT_MAX}. The mix axis (+0x630) is built with
#                                  0.125 and the phase axis (+0x634) with 0.15, and both start
#                                  centred at a half — which is what makes a body that is not being
#                                  steered sit in the middle of each row of the grid

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# V14 — how a clip *name* becomes a file. One animation set for the whole game, keyed by name, so the
# first set to claim a name decides what every motion object built later gets. The port is the
# `claimed`/`claims`/`claim` additions in crates/game-render/src/anim.rs and the entry is
# research/journal/2026-10-17-v14-the-grid-rows-third-column.
anim_mcf_motion boot 0x00142220 (set in a0, the motion-set object in a1, the ini section in a2) one
#                               `[MOTION n]` of a `.mcf`. Reads `Name=` (default "NONE"), calls
#                               anim_clip_by_name and **returns that clip untouched when it exists**:
#                               `VUE=`, `Looping` and `TransitionTime` are all inside the not-found
#                               arm, so a later `.mcf` that names a clip someone else has claimed
#                               changes neither its file nor its loop bit. The file is the object's
#                               content directory (+0x1e0) joined to `VUE=`, with everything from the
#                               first '.' replaced by ".MOT". Its sibling at 0x00142e10 is the same
#                               shape for the set's second list (+0x1c count, +0x20 array)
#   the key strings: 0x00426ed0 "Name", 0x00426ed8 "NONE", 0x00426ee0 "VUE", 0x00426ef0 "Looping",
#   0x00426ef8 ".MOT", 0x00426f00 "TransitionTime", 0x00426f10 ".vue", 0x00426ee8 "" (the path buffer's
#   initial byte). anim_find_or_load 0x00142430 builds "<+0x1e0><name>" and forces ".MOT" the same
#   way, and never reads `VUE=` at all -- which is why the 36 bare pose names of vehicle_load_clips
#   ignore aliases entirely
# V121: which set claims first is who is on the track. With AI riders out (V12's capture) the AI
#   body's `airider.mcf` claims the three grid aliases first; with none (S228's fly_and_collide, C11's
#   2p tape: two humans, no AI) the player's `mrider.mcf` does, and turn_stop_Fwd/Bck are their own
#   files, turn_still_Bck is turn_still_Mid.MOT. Proved by pose, not by reading the spawn path
#   (research/journal/2026-11-03-v121-grid-row-claims). The port's Body::load takes Set::alone.

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# V19 — the body's *mode* machine: the other slot of the rider body actor's vtable, and the only
# writer of the settle flag the S27 selection reads. The port is the Mode/Thrown/Machine additions in
# crates/game-sim/src/rider_anim.rs plus the two-line wiring in Rider::body_frame, the proof is the
# fifth test of crates/game-sim/tests/rider_anim.rs, and the entry is
# research/journal/2026-10-17-v19-a-wreck-that-settles.
rider_body_mode boot 0x0024c7e0 (3592 bytes; dt in f12, the actor in a1) one frame of what is *posing*
#                               the rider's body, run from the body actor's update (boot 0x0024e640,
#                               its one caller) before the selection. Picks one of five modes into
#                               +0x47c, runs the entry of whichever it has just changed into (+0x480
#                               is the previous one), and in the wreck mode runs the clocks and the
#                               test that decides a thrown body has come to rest.
#   the five modes, in the order the chain tests them: 0 the request's own +0xc placement flag is set
#   (whoever owns the request is placing the body), 2 the request's wreck reason +0x104 is non-zero,
#   3 the step's from_table/holding (a backflip raises the second), 1 riding (the request's cut flag
#   +0x140 clear and no look-around idle), 4 the start line or a look-around. The entry chain is
#   `if 0 / else if 4 / else if 2 / else if (2 || prev != 2) { if 3 / else if 1 } / else <cleanup>`,
#   so the arm that cleans a wreck up is the last else and a body leaving the wreck for 0 or 4 is
#   cleaned up by neither -- those two match first
#   the wreck entry is boot 0x0024ad90 (5504 bytes), handed the reason: it puts the ragdoll where the
#   bike is, hands every bone the bike's velocity and the limp damping, and draws five numbers out of
#   the throw -- the spin's three lanes, then +0x6bc, then +0x6c0, each `base + delta * (2u - 1)`
#   the body's wreck fields, by offset (a test may say where these live; the port may not):
#     +0x48c  something of the ragdoll is touching    +0x490  how long this roll has lasted, seconds
#     +0x6b4  the body has come to rest (S27's flag)  +0x6b8  it was flung off the ground again
#     +0x6bc  what is left of the throw's ForceTime   +0x6c0  ... of its HandHoldTime
#     +0x6d0  the randomised spin, as a quaternion    +0x6e0  the orientation it is thrown at
#     +0x6a8/+0x6ac  which frame that is taken in     +0x6b0  the bones are off the bike
#     +0x648  a clock counted down in the wreck       +0x620/+0x624 the bone count and array
#     +0x8c0  the damping record handed to the main bone's vtable +0x54 on the settle
#   the rider file's [Wrecks] lives on this actor at +0x700: seven 0x40-stride throw records
#   (ForceTime +0x00, HandHoldTime +0x04, Force +0x10, ForceTimeDelta +0x20, HandHoldTimeDelta +0x24,
#   ForceDelta +0x30), then +0x8c0 GroundAngularRateDamping as a quadword, +0x8d0 RiderFlailHeight,
#   +0x8d4 RiderRollTime, +0x8d8 RiderRollSpeed -- which is exactly game_sim::phy::Wrecks
#   **the settle test**, after ground_query (boot 0x001dc960: world +0x2bc, a copy of the main bone's
#   +0x60, no normal, flags or record) with the main bone at [[+0x2f4]+0xa4] (+0x64 its height,
#   +0x70/+0x74/+0x78 its velocity), touching = any of the +0x620/+0x624 bone collisions' +0x14 (S52):
#     if (roll != 0 || 0 < loose || 0 < thrown || flung || height - ground_y <= 0x8d0)
#        if (roll <= 0 || !flung)
#           if ((0x8d4 < roll || |velocity| < 0x8d8) && !settled)  settled = 1, the bones take the
#              stiffer damping (boot 0x0024c310 with a1 = 1, set in a delay slot) and the main bone is
#              handed +0x8c0
#        else  the bones go limp (0x0024c310 with a1 = 0) and flung clears
#     else  the bones go stiff, flung = 1 and settled = 0
#   so a wrecked body settles the frame its roll has lasted RiderRollTime *or* its speed has dropped
#   below RiderRollSpeed, guarded by an outer "not still being thrown about" and an inner condition
#   that separates a landed body from a just-flung one. The square root is taken inside the second
#   test only, which is why the port takes it lazily
#   its ten callees, named here so a test can hook them: the five the entries reach and the five the
#   wreck arm does. The wreck frame proved in crates/game-sim/tests/rider_anim.rs hooks all of them
#   and lets only the mode chain and the settle test run
rider_body_wreck_enter boot 0x0024ad90 (5504 bytes) the wreck's entry: the ragdoll is put where the
#                                      bike is and the throw's five numbers are drawn
rider_body_damping boot 0x0024c310 (372 bytes, the actor in a0 and which set in a1) hands every bone
#                                  one of the two damping records stored beside it -- a1 = 1 is the
#                                  stiffer set, 0 the limp one
rider_body_look_reset boot 0x0024d5f0 (240 bytes) the look-around and placement reset the Held, Gate
#                                     and Posed entries run
rider_body_ride_enter boot 0x0024c490 (836 bytes) the riding mode's own entry
rider_ragdoll_step boot 0x001dc960 (1300 bytes) **misnamed by V19: this is ground_query** (above).
#                                  The wreck arm copies the main bone's position, ground-queries the
#                                  copy and tests main bone y - ground y <= RiderFlailHeight: a
#                                  clearance above the ground, not a rise across a step (S50). Since
#                                  S52 tests/rider_anim.rs hooks it as ground_query; the name is kept
#                                  only so that older journals still resolve
rider_bone_follow boot 0x00218b80 (976 bytes) points a dummy at a bone
rider_bone_post boot 0x001f6840 (20 bytes) the per-bone call of the arm's tail
rider_bone_detach boot 0x0011aed0 (72 bytes) takes one bone off the bike it was following
rand_raw boot 0x0040fd40 (100 bytes) the generator behind every draw, as an int
quat_axes boot 0x0013f380 (132 bytes) a quaternion's axes, which the look arm reads
# S50 — the ragdoll's two entries (crates/game-sim/src/ragdoll.rs, proved in tests/ragdoll.rs)
rider_ragdoll_pose boot 0x0030f0c0 the body actor's vt +0xa4 (actor, a1, dt in f12): every bone body
#                                  put at its skeleton bone's world transform, velocity from the bone's
#                                  move over dt (zero when dt <= 0). S52 ported it, given the
#                                  skeleton's pose (ragdoll_pose::pose): per part (+0x2f4, stride 0xb0)
#                                  node +0xa0 -> node_world_position and orientation_axes (relative to
#                                  null), q = axes_to_quat on the w >= 0 side, then (unless a1 or
#                                  dt <= 0) velocity = (position - part +0x90..+0x98) / dt and rates
#                                  = quat_axis_angle(unturn(q, rel(q, mat4_to_quat(part +0x60)))) as
#                                  axis * angle / dt; body vt +0x10 (position + turn(q, body +0x180),
#                                  velocity), vt +0x48 (q, rates), part +0x60 = mat4_basis(position,
#                                  forward, up, 0, 1)
rider_ragdoll_reset boot 0x0030f0b0 the actor's vt +0xa0: the world reset (0x001f28a0), each body's
#                                  vt +0x2c (0x0015a3d0)
# S201 -- the riding entry after a put-back (journal 2026-11-02-s201-riding-entry). Held ticks: the
#   still repose then the reset, so every part +0x60 = mat4_basis(body centre = node + turn(q, cofg)),
#   placed +0x2ac = 0. T+2's entry pose (moving) reads the nodes T+1 left (the animation has not yet
#   written this tick's), so each start +0x30 = T+1's position bit for bit and +0x40 = (node - part
#   +0x90)/dt = -turn(q, cofg)/dt; the reset copies them in before the step. Hand pins at T+2 already
#   follow this tick's holds (+0x4d0..) and +0x530; the box frame +0x580 is this tick's.
# S52 -- the reset, ported in crates/game-sim/src/ragdoll_pose.rs (proved in tests/ragdoll_contact.rs)
world_reset boot 0x001f28a0 (world) every body of the world (count +0x8, array +0x10), in order, through
#                           its vt (at the body's +0x100) +0x2c
body_reset boot 0x0015a3d0 (body) +0x50 and +0x60 = start position +0x30, +0x70 = start velocity +0x40,
#                          +0x150 and +0x170 = start quat +0x160, +0x1b0 = start rates +0x140; +0x80,
#                          +0x90, +0xa0, +0xb0, +0xc0, +0x190, +0x1a0, +0x1c0, +0x1d0, +0x1e0 = (0,0,0,1)
body_set_start boot 0x00159f20 (body, pos*, vel*) vt +0x10: +0x30 = pos, +0x40 = vel (whole quads)
body_set_start_turn boot 0x00159ee0 (body, quat*, rates*) vt +0x48: +0x160 = quat, +0x140 = rates
node_world_position boot 0x001b2870 (node, relative_to, out) a skeleton node's world position (the
#                                  pose's first read; hooked in tests/ragdoll_contact.rs)
axes_to_quat boot 0x0013f9f0 (out, forward*, up*) mat4_to_quat of mat4_basis((0,0,0,1), forward, up,
#                           0, 1)
quat_axis_angle boot 0x0013f110 (q*, angle*, axis*) w clamped to -1..1 (a w that does not compare
#                              is 1), a = the acos table at it; a == 0 -> axis (1,0,0,1). Otherwise
#                              axis = q.xyz / the curve table's raw sin entry at wrap(wrap(a)), w = 1,
#                              angle = 2a
collider_ignore boot 0x0011ade0 (228 bytes; collider, other) adds other to the collider's ignore list
#                               (+0xd4 array, +0xd8 count) once, into the first empty slot
wreck_sound boot 0x0033e9c0 (524 bytes; emitter, a1) the wreck entry's last call, on actor +0xa74:

# S37 — the wreck test's contact clause (reason 6). bike_wreck_test's last loop reads the bike's
# collision record (bike+0x7d0): +0x40 "anything touched" (the word air::Step::touching is), +0x100
# the contact count, +0x104 the capacity (11 on a bike, one per body ray), +0x108 the list, 0x80 bytes
# an entry: +0x00 kind (0 = found by this record's own rays, 1 = mirrored in by another record's),
# +0x10 the ray's start and +0x20 its end in the bike's model space (so +0x24 is how high the ray
# reaches), +0x30 normal, +0x40 point, +0x70 a word the writer is handed, +0x74 the other actor's
# record (0 = scenery), +0x78 another. The first own entry reaching above 6 (4 with bike+0x588) is the
# only one read: scenery enters reason 6; an actor with a chassis (+0xf0, height at +0x64, velocity
# at +0x260) enters 7 if that chassis is over bike+0x784 higher; one without (+0xf4 its rigid body,
# mass +0xc, velocity +0x70) enters 7 if heavier than the bike's chassis (+0x210). Either 7 copies the
# velocity to bike+0x560 first. The loop runs after the landing clauses and is not gated by bike+0x748,
# but it never enters a second wreck on the same call: the entry (bike_wreck_entry) unfiles the record
# (0x0026f720 -> its vt+0x74, collision_record_clear: +0x100 and +0x40..+0x4c zeroed), and the loop
# re-reads +0x7d0 and asks +0x40 first (S191a; the Ee harnesses that hook vt+0xfc never saw it).
# bike+0x588 is set by the bike constructor when its name contains "Race50" or "Pro50" (strstr).
# The bike's eleven body rays: record+0x1f0's object, +0x168 count, +0x170 (start, end) pairs, all
# from one hub at (0, 4.0637, -0.0968); ray 8 ends at (0, 6.6743, 0.1712), the only end above 6, and
# rays 0 and 1 at y 4.58, the other two above 4.

machine_placement boot 0x0020f6e0 "%s%s\machine.txt" [Machines] NumberOfMachines; for a player whose
#                                 event vehicle kind (event +0x11c + i*0xc) is 1 it reads
#                                 "Biplane%d" (i+1): Type, Position, Direction, HasPlayer, HasAI,
#                                 MachineRacer, SetGroundHeight; else "Machine%d". free06:
#                                 [Biplane1] 2304,135,2281 dir 0,0,-1
#                                 S164: SetGroundHeight (default 1, key string 0x42edb0) puts
#                                 Position on the ground: ground_query on the point itself (x, z
#                                 kept); off, the query runs on a copy and only its normal is used.
#                                 The normal is the spawn's up. vehicle_spawn then lifts the point:
#                                 0x001b44b0(model node +0x18c, &centre, &half) reads the node's
#                                 merged bounds (+0xe0 centre, +0xf0 half; node_bounds 0x001b3f50
#                                 over the whole tree) and pos.y += centre.y; vt(+0x88)+0xe0
#                                 (0x0015a180) stores (pos, dir, up) at +0xe0/+0xf0/+0x100, copied to
#                                 the spawn +0xe10.., and physics_object_place 0x0015a1b0 (vt +0xe4)
#                                 puts the BODY (the centre of gravity) on pos, not the model origin.
#                                 free06: ground 134.9986 (0x4306ffa4) + biplane.psg centre y
#                                 3.6154737 = 138.61406 (0x430a9d33), the captured body y; body
#                                 (2304, 138.614, 2281), model origin (2304, 135.114, 2278.5).
#                                 mxbiplane_reset (0x00355b00) and the chord put the body back there
#                                 through 0x00351df0 -> 0x0015a030 -> vt +0xe4 on the same three.
#                                 S205: the normal goes to vehicle_spawn's up untouched (the query's
#                                 auStack_a40, both branches), and vt +0xe0 stores it raw; the first
#                                 vt +0xe4 is handed the factory's own copies, every reset the stored
#                                 three, which physics_object_place squares in place (so the stored nose
#                                 and up are the squared ones after the first reset). Port:
#                                 Flight::spawn_up, flight::spawn_axes (proved by game-sim
#                                 tests/spawn_axes.rs over 10,000 inputs). SetGroundHeight off: the
#                                 query runs on a copy (x, y, z of the file, w of the factory's), and
#                                 only the normal is used. Disc: every [Biplane1/2] on free06/07/09/10/
#                                 11 is on flat ground (normal 0, 0.99999994, 0) and none sets
#                                 SetGroundHeight; free06's [Machine1] is the one section with it
#                                 (FALSE). Journal 2026-11-02-s205-biplane-spawn-up.

biplane_input           boot 0x00351e20 (820 bytes; vtable +0x20 through the thunk 0x00355f40) the eight

#   0x38c/0x390/0x394), +0x35c aileron (toward +0x344 x MaxAileron at +0x370), +0x360 rudder (toward
